```
1	# git-sync
2	
3	`git-sync` mirrors refs from a source remote to a target remote without creating a local checkout. It uses an in-memory `go-git` object store and talks smart HTTP directly:
4	
5	- `info/refs` ref advertisement for source and target
6	- `upload-pack` fetch from source with target tip hashes advertised as `have`
7	- `receive-pack` push to target with explicit ref update commands and a streamed packfile
8	
9	That keeps the target side incremental without fetching target objects into the local process first.
10	
11	## Why This Exists
12	
13	Git already has pieces of this problem, but not this exact tool shape.
14	
15	What usually exists today:
16	
17	- a full local `git clone --mirror` followed by `git push --mirror`
18	- host-specific import or migration features
19	- CI jobs or shell scripts that glue fetch and push steps together
20	- one-off migration tooling tied to a specific platform
21	
22	What those approaches usually do not give you:
23	
24	- direct remote-to-remote relay behavior
25	- a small standalone CLI with explicit sync semantics
26	- front-loaded validation and planning
27	- machine-readable output for automation
28	- one tool that covers empty-target bootstrap, normal sync, and large-repo bootstrap fallback
29	
30	That is the gap `git-sync` is trying to fill.
31	
32	The main value is operational:
33	
34	- avoid requiring a full local mirror checkout just to move refs between remotes
35	- make initial seeding of large repositories cheaper and more predictable
36	- keep incremental sync behavior explicit and safe
37	- give operators and automation a stable way to inspect, plan, execute, and benchmark the same workflows
38	
39	This is especially useful when:
40	
41	- the target is a new hosted Git service or internal Git endpoint
42	- bootstrap size matters more than local developer ergonomics
43	- you want a repeatable machine-oriented sync primitive rather than an ad hoc migration script
44	- you need clearer control over mapping, pruning, force rules, and relay behavior than generic shell glue usually provides
45	
46	Compared to a service that keeps persistent local clones, `git-sync` is the better fit when:
47	
48	- relay is common enough that streaming source-to-target is the normal case
49	- avoiding persistent local repo storage is an operational advantage
50	- remote-to-remote efficiency matters more than full local Git generality
51	
52	If you need arbitrary complex reconciliation through one always-warm local full-state model, a local-clone service is still the more general tool.
53	
54	The command surface is:
55	
56	- `git-sync probe`: inspect a source remote, and optionally a target remote
57	- `git-sync fetch`: exercise source-side fetch negotiation without pushing
58	- `git-sync bootstrap`: seed an empty target with create-only relay behavior
59	- `git-sync plan`: compute source-to-target ref actions without pushing, with `--mode sync|replicate`
60	- `git-sync sync`: execute the planned changes against the target
61	- `git-sync replicate`: execute source-authoritative relay-only replication against the target
62	- `git-sync-bench`: run repeatable benchmark scenarios against fresh empty targets
63	
64	## Library API
65	
66	`git-sync` now has a two-tier Go API:
67	
68	- `pkg/gitsync`
69	  - stable embedding surface for queue workers and other external callers
70	  - typed `Probe`, `Plan`, `Sync`, and `Replicate` requests/results
71	  - injected auth and HTTP client support
72	- `pkg/gitsync/unstable`
73	  - explicitly non-stable surface for first-party tooling and advanced controls
74	  - includes `Bootstrap`, `Fetch`, batching and measurement knobs, and CLI-oriented execution options
75	
76	If you are embedding `git-sync` outside this repo, prefer `pkg/gitsync`. The CLI and benchmark command use `pkg/gitsync/unstable` because they still need direct access to advanced engine controls that are intentionally not part of the stable API.
77	
78	The stable `pkg/gitsync` results are shaped for workers:
79	
80	- `Refs`
81	  - per-ref outcomes
82	- `Counts`
83	  - aggregate applied/skipped/blocked/deleted counts
84	- `Execution`
85	  - execution mode, protocol, relay summary, and batch summary
86	
87	See [docs/embedding.md](docs/embedding.md) for worker-oriented guidance.
88	
89	## Current scope
90	
91	- Smart HTTP only
92	- No local working tree
93	- Branch mirroring by default
94	- Optional tag mirroring with `--tags`
95	- Optional exact ref mapping with `--map`
96	- Fast-forward safety by default
97	- Optional forced retargeting with `--force`
98	- Optional source-authoritative relay-only replication with `replicate` / `plan --mode replicate`
99	- Optional managed-ref deletion with `--prune`
100	- Optional transfer stats output with `--stats`
101	- Optional machine-readable output with `--json`
102	- Optional source-side Git protocol v2 for `ls-refs` and `fetch`
103	
104	## Limits
105	
106	- Push still uses the existing v1-style `receive-pack` path.
107	- Protocol v2 support currently covers source discovery and source fetch only.
108	- `--protocol auto` tries source-side v2 first and falls back to v1.
109	- `--protocol v2` requires the source remote to negotiate v2.
110	- Ref mapping is explicit, not wildcard-based.
111	- Only smart HTTP remotes are supported.
112	- Objects are kept in memory for the duration of the run.
113	- Non-relay materialized syncs are bounded by `--materialized-max-objects`, an object-count guardrail for the in-memory fallback path.
114	
115	## Quick Start
116	
117	```bash
118	go run ./cmd/git-sync sync \
119	  --source-token "$GITSYNC_SOURCE_TOKEN" \
120	  --target-token "$GITSYNC_TARGET_TOKEN" \
121	  https://github.com/source-org/source-repo.git \
122	  https://github.com/target-org/target-repo.git
123	```
124	
125	## Commands
126	
127	Plan a sync without pushing anything:
128	
129	```bash
130	go run ./cmd/git-sync plan \
131	  --stats \
132	  https://github.com/source-org/source-repo.git \
133	  https://github.com/target-org/target-repo.git
134	```
135	
136	Plan a source-authoritative replication without pushing anything:
137	
138	```bash
139	go run ./cmd/git-sync plan \
140	  --mode replicate \
141	  --stats \
142	  https://github.com/source-org/source-repo.git \
143	  https://github.com/target-org/target-repo.git
144	```
145	
146	Execute relay-only replication that overwrites differing managed refs and fails instead of materializing locally:
147	
148	```bash
149	go run ./cmd/git-sync replicate \
150	  --stats \
151	  https://github.com/source-org/source-repo.git \
152	  https://github.com/target-org/target-repo.git
153	```
154	
155	If `replicate` cannot use relay against the target, it fails and tells you to rerun with `sync`.
156	
157	Bootstrap an empty target without using the normal local object-store sync path:
158	
159	```bash
160	go run ./cmd/git-sync bootstrap \
161	  --stats \
162	  https://github.com/source-org/source-repo.git \
163	  https://github.com/target-org/target-repo.git
164	```
165	
166	Add `--max-pack-bytes` to abort bootstrap if the streamed source pack grows past a safety threshold:
167	
168	```bash
169	go run ./cmd/git-sync bootstrap \
170	  --max-pack-bytes 104857600 \
171	  <source-url> \
172	  <target-url>
173	```
174	
175	Add `--target-max-pack-bytes` to split large branch bootstraps into multiple relay batches with temporary refs:
176	
177	```bash
178	go run ./cmd/git-sync bootstrap \
179	  --target-max-pack-bytes 1073741824 \
180	  <source-url> \
181	  <target-url>
182	```
183	
184	Current batching scope is intentionally narrow:
185	
186	- protocol v2 only
187	- branch refs are batched
188	- optional create-only tags are pushed after branch batches complete
189	- temporary refs under `refs/gitsync/bootstrap/heads/`
190	- resume from existing temp refs is supported when they match a planned checkpoint
191	
192	This mode is intended as an advanced large-repo fallback, not the default bootstrap path. Use plain `bootstrap` first when a single streamed initial sync is acceptable.
193	
194	A practical starting point is:
195	
196	- `--target-max-pack-bytes 536870912` for a conservative `512 MiB` target-side batch size
197	- `--target-max-pack-bytes 1073741824` when you want fewer, larger batches and the target has more headroom
198	
199	For example:
200	
201	```bash
202	go run ./cmd/git-sync bootstrap \
203	  --target-max-pack-bytes 536870912 \
204	  --protocol v2 \
205	  -v \
206	  <source-url> \
207	  <target-url>
208	```
209	
210	Add `--measure-memory` to `bootstrap`, `sync`, `plan`, `probe`, or `fetch` to sample elapsed time and Go heap usage:
211	
212	```bash
213	go run ./cmd/git-sync bootstrap \
214	  --measure-memory \
215	  --json \
216	  <source-url> \
217	  <target-url>
218	```
219	
220	That is useful for one-off measurements on the same fixture or test repo.
221	
222	## Benchmarking
223	
224	For repeated benchmark runs, prefer the dedicated benchmark command instead of manually wrapping `git-sync` invocations:
225	
226	```bash
227	go run ./cmd/git-sync-bench \
228	  --scenario bootstrap \
229	  --source-url /tmp/git-sync-bench/kubernetes.git \
230	  --repeat 3 \
231	  --target-max-pack-bytes 104857600 \
232	  --stats \
233	  --json
234	```
235	
236	`git-sync-bench` creates a fresh bare target repository for each run, executes the selected scenario in-process, and reports:
237	
238	- per-run wall-clock time
239	- per-run `syncer.Result`
240	- aggregate min/avg/max wall time
241	- aggregate internal elapsed and heap metrics from `--measure-memory`
242	- relay modes observed across successful runs
243	
244	If `--source-url` is a local path, it is converted to `file://...` automatically. The current scenarios are:
245	
246	- `--scenario bootstrap`
247	- `--scenario sync`
248	
249	For large-repo measurements, use a local bare mirror as the source so the benchmark reflects `git-sync` behavior rather than internet variance. See [docs/benchmarking.md](docs/benchmarking.md) for details.
250	
251	## Sync Behavior
252	
253	When `sync` sees that all managed target refs are absent and the run is compatible with bootstrap semantics, it automatically uses the bootstrap relay path instead of the normal decode-and-repack sync path.
254	
255	`sync` also uses a narrow incremental relay path for fast-forward branch updates and tag creation when there is no prune/delete, no force, and the target does not advertise `no-thin`. This now includes multi-branch batches, branch-to-branch mappings, and create-only tags. Tag retargeting and other more complex updates still fall back to the normal local decode-and-repack path.
256	
257	If `sync` falls back to the materialized path, `--materialized-max-objects` sets an explicit object-count safety bound for the in-memory object set. It is a conservative guardrail, not a precise heap-size limit.
258	
259	Sync specific branches:
260	
261	```bash
262	go run ./cmd/git-sync sync \
263	  --branch main,release \
264	  --source-token "$GITSYNC_SOURCE_TOKEN" \
265	  --target-token "$GITSYNC_TARGET_TOKEN" \
266	  <source-url> \
267	  <target-url>
268	```
269	
270	Map a source branch to a different target branch:
271	
272	```bash
273	go run ./cmd/git-sync sync \
274	  --map main:stable \
275	  <source-url> \
276	  <target-url>
277	```
278	
279	Mirror tags and prune managed target refs that disappeared from source:
280	
281	```bash
282	go run ./cmd/git-sync sync \
283	  --tags \
284	  --prune \
285	  <source-url> \
286	  <target-url>
287	```
288	
289	Force source-side protocol v2:
290	
291	```bash
292	go run ./cmd/git-sync sync \
293	  --protocol v2 \
294	  <source-url> \
295	  <target-url>
296	```
297	
298	Probe a source remote without pushing anything:
299	
300	```bash
301	go run ./cmd/git-sync probe \
302	  --stats \
303	  --tags \
304	  --protocol auto \
305	  <source-url>
306	```
307	
308	Probe both source and target remotes to inspect source fetch capabilities and target `receive-pack` capabilities:
309	
310	```bash
311	go run ./cmd/git-sync probe \
312	  --stats \
313	  <source-url> \
314	  <target-url>
315	```
316	
317	Fetch from a source remote into memory without pushing anywhere:
318	
319	```bash
320	go run ./cmd/git-sync fetch \
321	  --stats \
322	  --protocol auto \
323	  --branch main \
324	  <source-url>
325	```
326	
327	Advertise an existing source ref as a synthetic `have` to exercise incremental negotiation:
328	
329	```bash
330	go run ./cmd/git-sync fetch \
331	  --stats \
332	  --protocol auto \
333	  --branch main \
334	  --have-ref main \
335	  <source-url>
336	```
337	
338	Dry run:
339	
340	```bash
341	go run ./cmd/git-sync plan --stats <source-url> <target-url>
342	```
343	
344	## JSON Output
345	
346	Add `--json` to `probe`, `fetch`, `bootstrap`, `plan`, or `sync` to emit machine-readable output instead of the default text format.
347	
348	The JSON interface is intentionally stable:
349	
350	- keys use `snake_case`
351	- refs and hashes are serialized as strings, not raw byte arrays
352	- `probe` returns top-level keys such as `source_url`, `target_url`, `protocol`, `ref_prefixes`, `source_capabilities`, `target_capabilities`, `refs`, and `stats`
353	- `fetch` returns top-level keys such as `source_url`, `protocol`, `wants`, `haves`, `fetched_objects`, and `stats`
354	- `bootstrap`, `plan`, and `sync` return top-level keys such as `plans`, `pushed`, `skipped`, `blocked`, `deleted`, `dry_run`, `protocol`, and `stats`
355	- `bootstrap`, `plan`, and `sync` also expose `relay`, `relay_mode`, `relay_reason`, `batching`, `batch_count`, `planned_batch_count`, and `temp_refs`
356	- each item in `plans` includes stable string fields such as `branch`, `source_ref`, `target_ref`, `source_hash`, `target_hash`, `kind`, `action`, and `reason`
357	
358	## Auth
359	
360	For GitHub and similar providers, use basic auth with a token as the password.
361	
362	Auth is resolved in this order:
363	
364	- explicit CLI flags
365	- `GITSYNC_*` environment variables
366	- local `git credential fill` helper lookup for `http` and `https` remotes
367	- anonymous access
368	
369	Relevant variables:
370	
371	- `GITSYNC_SOURCE_TOKEN`
372	- `GITSYNC_TARGET_TOKEN`
373	- `GITSYNC_SOURCE_USERNAME` default: `git`
374	- `GITSYNC_TARGET_USERNAME` default: `git`
375	
376	Bearer auth is also available:
377	
378	- `GITSYNC_SOURCE_BEARER_TOKEN`
379	- `GITSYNC_TARGET_BEARER_TOKEN`
380	
381	That means local testing against a dummy GitHub repo can reuse your regular Git credential helper setup without passing tokens on every command.
382	
383	## Protocol Notes
384	
385	- Source refs are listed with `GET /info/refs?service=git-upload-pack`.
386	- When the source supports it, the client can negotiate protocol v2 with `Git-Protocol: version=2`, then use `ls-refs` and `fetch`.
387	- Target refs are listed with `GET /info/refs?service=git-receive-pack`.
388	- The source fetch advertises current target tip hashes as `have`, so reruns download less when source and target already share history.
389	- Target push stays on the current `receive-pack` path.
390	- If a target ref does not exist, it is created.
391	- If a target ref already matches the source, it is skipped.
392	- Branches are updated only when the target tip is an ancestor of the source tip, unless `--force` is set.
393	- Tags are immutable by default. Retargeting an existing tag requires `--force`.
394	- If `--prune` is set, managed target refs that are absent on source are deleted.
395	- `plan` never pushes. If `sync` finds blocked refs, it exits non-zero before pushing anything.
396	- `--stats` adds per-service request, byte, want, have, and command counters to the output.
397	
398	Push still uses the current low-level `receive-pack` path. Protocol v2 is used where it materially improves this tool: source-side ref discovery and source-side object download.
399	
400	## Testing
401	
402	Default suite:
403	
404	```bash
405	env GOCACHE=/tmp/go-build go test ./...
406	```
407	
408	Extended and environment-specific test instructions are in [docs/testing.md](docs/testing.md).
409	
410	## Design Notes
411	
412	`bootstrap` is the dedicated path for large initial syncs into an empty target. The goal is to relay a fetched source pack directly into target `receive-pack` instead of decoding the full object graph into local memory first.
413	
414	Current architectural summary and package boundaries are in [docs/architecture.md](docs/architecture.md).
415	
416	The design note is in [docs/bootstrap.md](docs/bootstrap.md).
417	
418	For very large single-branch repositories, there is also a batching design and initial implementation note in [docs/bootstrap-batching.md](docs/bootstrap-batching.md).
419

> AGENT

Short answer: technically yes, but not in the streaming-relay sense git-sync is built around — it becomes a full materialize-translate-repack path, and it breaks signatures.

Why it isn't cheap:

1. **Object contents embed hashes.** Trees list entry SHA-1s, commits list parent/tree SHA-1s, tags list target SHA-1s. To produce SHA-256 objects you must decode every object, rewrite internal references, and rehash. That kills the relay optimization in `bootstrap`/`sync` — you can't just forward the source packfile bytes (internal/relay is the current fast path; see `docs/bootstrap.md`).

2. **You need a persistent bidirectional mapping.** For incremental runs, target tips are SHA-256 but the source's `have` negotiation expects SHA-1. git-sync would have to carry (or re-derive) a SHA-1↔SHA-256 map across runs, or re-translate everything each time. Git core handles this via `extensions.compatObjectFormat` / the "transition plan" loose-object mapping — doing it outside git means reimplementing that table.

3. **Signed commits and signed tags break.** The signature covers the object bytes. Rewriting references changes the bytes, so every GPG/SSH signature becomes invalid. There's no fix-up; this is inherent to content-addressed storage.

4. **Memory/cost profile changes.** Current `--materialized-max-objects` guardrail already bounds the fallback in-memory path. A translating push is strictly a materialized path plus a rewrite pass and a mapping table, so it's more expensive than today's worst case, not less.

5. **Receive-pack on a SHA-256 target speaks SHA-256 wire format.** That's a protocol switch too (the push path is still v1-style here; wire-level object IDs, ref advertisement, capabilities like `object-format=sha256` all change). So the push side needs a second codepath, not a flag flip.

Practical take: it would be a separate `--translate-to sha256` mode that replaces relay with decode-rewrite-rehash-repack, persists a hash map alongside managed refs, and documents that signatures don't survive. Worth it if the goal is a one-shot migration to a SHA-256 backend; not worth grafting onto the incremental relay path.

If you want, I can sketch where this would slot into the engine (plan → materialized translate stage → new push codec) without writing code yet.

> DEVELOPER

different question: if the target supports SHA256 could it translate on receive? is that a thing?

> AGENT

Yes — this is actually a thing in git core, called the **hash function transition / interop mode**, using `extensions.compatObjectFormat`.

How it works server-side:

1. The target repo is created with `objectFormat = sha256` and `compatObjectFormat = sha1` (or vice versa). Git then stores a bidirectional hash map for every object it receives.
2. On `receive-pack`, if the client pushes SHA-1 wire format (negotiated via the `object-format` capability), the server translates incoming objects into its primary format and records the mapping. Trees/commits are rewritten so internal references point at the primary-format hashes; the original form is recoverable through the map.
3. On `upload-pack`/ref advertisement, the server can serve either format to a client depending on which `object-format=` the client negotiates. SHA-1 clients see SHA-1 hashes; SHA-256 clients see SHA-256.
4. Signed commits/tags: the transition plan handles this by signing in a canonical form and carrying the signature + original bytes; interop is designed so signatures still verify under the hash they were made in. (In practice this is the most brittle part and the bit operators hit issues with.)

The upshot for git-sync:

- **If the target server actually implements compat mode**, git-sync needs no translation code at all. You just push SHA-1 as today; the server does the work. git-sync's relay path even stays intact, because the wire bytes can flow through unchanged until they hit the server.
- You do need to negotiate `object-format=sha1` in the push capabilities, and confirm the server advertises `object-format` at all. Today git-sync's `receive-pack` path doesn't inspect that; it would want to, just to fail loudly if the target is SHA-256-only with no compat.
- For incremental reruns, target ref advertisement comes back in whatever format the client asked for, so the `have` list stays SHA-1 and negotiation keeps working.

Current reality check (worth verifying against today's forges before relying on it):

- Git core has had this since ~2.42, marked experimental.
- GitLab has had some SHA-256 repo support; GitHub historically has not shipped SHA-256 repos.
- Gitea/Forgejo have varying support.
- Self-hosted git daemons are the most likely place you'd actually encounter a SHA-256 target with compat mode.

So: "translate on receive" is the right architectural layer for this problem, and it already exists in git — it's just a server capability question, not a git-sync feature request. The sensible thing for git-sync is to detect and surface it (probe `object-format` capabilities; refuse a SHA-256-only push target with a clear error) rather than try to implement translation itself.

> DEVELOPER

and if we pick up sync later this would also work?

> AGENT

Yes, later incremental sync works, as long as two conditions hold on the target:

1. **Compat mode is permanent, not bootstrap-only.** The SHA-1↔SHA-256 mapping has to stay enabled for the life of the repo, not just the initial import. Git does persist the map alongside objects, so this is the default once `compatObjectFormat` is set — but if an operator ever disables it later, the SHA-1 view disappears and your next sync has nothing to negotiate against.

2. **git-sync always negotiates `object-format=sha1` with the target.** Both on the `info/refs?service=git-receive-pack` advertisement and on the push itself. That's what makes the server return ref tips as SHA-1 hashes (translated through the map) instead of its native SHA-256 form. Without this, target advertisement comes back as SHA-256 and is useless as `have` input to a SHA-1 source.

Given those, the incremental flow stays identical to today:

- Probe target → get SHA-1 tips (via compat map).
- Advertise those as `have` to source upload-pack.
- Source returns a thin SHA-1 pack of only new objects.
- Relay to target; server translates and extends the map.
- Fast-forward checks, `--prune`, `--force`, tag immutability — all operate on SHA-1 hashes on both sides, so the existing logic is untouched.

Edge cases that still behave correctly:

- **Third-party pushes to the target in SHA-256 between syncs.** The server computes compat SHA-1s for those objects too, so advertisement stays consistent. git-sync will see a SHA-1 tip it doesn't recognize; source won't have it as a `have` match, and the next fetch just won't benefit from it. No corruption, just a slightly less efficient negotiation.
- **Ref rewrites on target.** Hash comparison still works because both sides of the comparison are SHA-1.
- **Tag retargeting.** Same story — `--force` semantics are hash-based, format-agnostic.

One thing that does *not* work, and is worth flagging loudly: if compat mode was never enabled and the target is SHA-256-only, the first sync fails at capability negotiation, and no amount of later retrying fixes it without reconfiguring the target repo. So a sensible git-sync behavior would be to detect `object-format=sha256` without `object-format=sha1` in the receive capabilities on the very first probe and error out with a clear "target is SHA-256 only, enable `compatObjectFormat=sha1` or this tool can't sync from a SHA-1 source" message — rather than discovering it mid-push.

> AGENT

Yes, with one nuance: it's two invocations, not one config. The tool is one-directional per run by design — `source → target` with explicit scope — so you orchestrate the split externally:

```bash
# main: source is truth
git-sync sync --branch main  A  B

# feature branches: target-as-source, source-as-target (roles swap)
git-sync sync --branch feature/login,feature/billing  B  A
```

The scope flags already cover what you need per run:

- `--branch` filters which refs that invocation touches (cmd/git-sync/main.go:73).
- `--map src:dst` lets you rename during relay if the two sides use different conventions.
- `--prune` is per-invocation, so each direction only prunes refs it manages — the two runs don't step on each other as long as their `--branch` sets are disjoint.
- Fast-forward safety, `--force`, replicate mode, all behave the same regardless of which side you call "source."

The sharp edges to know about:

1. **No wildcard branches.** README is explicit: "Ref mapping is explicit, not wildcard-based." `feature/*` means enumerating branches, or listing them from a `probe` and piping into `--branch`. If bidirectional mixed-ownership becomes a common workflow, a `--branch-pattern 'feature/*'` flag on top of the existing planner filter is the obvious addition — small change, fits the existing scope model.

2. **Ownership discipline is on you.** Nothing in the tool prevents you from running both directions over overlapping branch sets and getting a ping-pong. The guardrail is your `--branch` list on each invocation. A lightweight config file that declares "these refs, this direction" and dispatches two runs would be a thin wrapper, not a core change — probably belongs in the caller (cron, CI, a small driver script), not in git-sync itself, to keep the CLI's semantics narrow.

3. **`replicate` is directional too.** If `main` should be strictly source-authoritative (overwrite target even on divergence), use `replicate` for that run; keep the feature-branch run as plain `sync` with fast-forward safety so target→source pushes from devs don't silently overwrite anything on the source side.

4. **Auth is per-run.** Both directions need credentials with push rights on their respective targets; `GITSYNC_SOURCE_TOKEN` / `GITSYNC_TARGET_TOKEN` get swapped between the two invocations. Trivial, but worth calling out if you're wiring this into CI secrets.

So: supported today for explicit branch lists; wants a glob flag if you want to express "all feature/\*" declaratively; fundamentally the right shape because the split-ownership policy lives in the operator's config, not inside the sync engine.

451	func planBootstrap(ctx context.Context, p Params, plans []planner.BranchPlan) ([]plannedBatch, error) {
452		planRefs := make([]planner.DesiredRef, 0, len(plans))
453		for _, plan := range plans {
454			if plan.Kind == planner.RefKindTag {
455				continue
456			}
457			if !plan.SourceRef.IsBranch() || !plan.TargetRef.IsBranch() {
458				return nil, fmt.Errorf("bootstrap batching currently supports branch refs and create-only tags")
459			}
460			ref, ok := p.DesiredRefs[plan.TargetRef]
461			if !ok {
462				return nil, fmt.Errorf("missing desired ref for %s", plan.TargetRef)
463			}
464			planRefs = append(planRefs, ref)
465		}
466		if len(planRefs) == 0 {
467			return nil, nil
468		}
469		p.log("bootstrap batch planning checkpoints", "branch_ref_count", len(planRefs), "planner", plannerName(p))
470		switch plannerName(p) {
471		case PlannerGraph:
472			return planBatches(ctx, p, planRefs)
473		case PlannerShallow:
474			return planBatchesShallow(ctx, p, planRefs)
475		default:
476			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unsupported bootstrap planner %q", p.Planner)
477		}
478	}

481		if p.Planner == "" {

542	// PlanCheckpoints plans the checkpoint hashes for a single branch during batched bootstrap.
543	func PlanCheckpoints(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {
544		var (
545			checkpoints []plumbing.Hash
546			err         error
547		)
548		switch plannerName(p) {
549		case PlannerGraph:
550			checkpoints, _, err = planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx, p, ref)
551		case PlannerShallow:
552			checkpoints, _, err = planCheckpointsShallow(ctx, p, ref)
553		default:
554			err = fmt.Errorf("unsupported bootstrap planner %q", p.Planner)
555		}
556		return checkpoints, err
557	}

559	// estimatedBytesPerCommit is the heuristic for estimating pack size from commit
560	// count. Real repos range from ~5 KiB/commit (small web apps) to ~120 KiB
561	// (blob-heavy monorepos); most mature repos fall in 20–80 KiB. 64 KiB produces
562	// accurate batch counts for large repos (linux is ~66 KiB/commit) while
563	// slightly overestimating for small ones (harmless — extra batches finish fast).
564	// The PACK header pre-check and target-rejection retry catch remaining error.
565	const estimatedBytesPerCommit = 65536
566	
567	func planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, []plumbing.Hash, error) {
568		p.log("bootstrap batch fetching commit graph", "branch", ref.TargetRef.String())
569	
570		// Fetch all commits (tree:0 filter) into a temporary in-memory store.
571		// For linux this is ~1.4M commits at ~3.3 KiB each = ~4.6 GB transient.
572		// We extract the first-parent chain (~75k hashes, ~3 MB) immediately
573		// and discard the store so GC can reclaim the 4.6 GB. The transient
574		// spike is unavoidable with git protocol v2 (no first-parent-only
575		// fetch) and go-git's pack parser (needs full store for delta resolution).
576		graphStore := memory.NewStorage()
577		gpRef := gitproto.DesiredRef{SourceRef: ref.SourceRef, TargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash}
578		if err := p.SourceService.FetchCommitGraph(ctx, graphStore, p.SourceConn, gpRef); err != nil {
579			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("fetch bootstrap planning graph for %s: %w", ref.TargetRef, err)
580		}
581		chain, err := planner.FirstParentChain(graphStore, ref.SourceHash)
582		graphStore = nil // allow GC to reclaim the commit graph store (~4.6 GB for linux)
583		runtime.GC()
584		if err != nil {
585			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("walk first-parent chain for %s: %w", ref.TargetRef, err)
586		}
587		if len(chain) == 0 {
588			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("empty first-parent chain for %s", ref.TargetRef)
589		}
590	
591		numBatches := estimateBatchCount(int64(len(chain)), p.TargetMaxPack)
592		checkpoints := evenCheckpoints(chain, numBatches)
593	
594		p.log("bootstrap batch planned checkpoints",
595			"branch", ref.TargetRef.String(),
596			"chain_len", len(chain),
597			"estimated_batches", len(checkpoints))
598	
599		return checkpoints, chain, nil
600	}
601	
602	func planCheckpointsShallow(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, []plumbing.Hash, error) {
603		if !p.SourceService.SupportsBootstrapBatch() {
604			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("bootstrap shallow planner requires protocol v2 source fetch filter support")
605		}
606		p.log("bootstrap shallow planner fetching ancestry slices", "branch", ref.TargetRef.String())
607		gpRef := gitproto.DesiredRef{SourceRef: ref.SourceRef, TargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash}
608		prevLen := 0
609		for depth := initialShallowDepth; depth <= maxShallowDepth; depth *= 2 {
610			graphStore := memory.NewStorage()
611			if err := p.SourceService.FetchCommitGraphDepth(ctx, graphStore, p.SourceConn, gpRef, depth); err != nil {
612				return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("fetch bootstrap shallow planning graph for %s depth=%d: %w", ref.TargetRef, depth, err)
613			}
614			chain, complete, err := firstParentPrefix(graphStore, ref.SourceHash)
615			graphStore = nil
616			runtime.GC()
617			if err != nil {
618				return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("walk shallow first-parent chain for %s depth=%d: %w", ref.TargetRef, depth, err)
619			}

> AGENT

Yes — and there's actually a bigger win hiding behind that suggestion. Ordering alone helps, but the real payoff is using the trunk's reachable set as a cutoff for every other branch's graph walk.

**What the log is showing.** `planCheckpointsFromChain` at internal/strategy/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:567 is called per branch in a plain loop (bootstrap.go:491). Each branch independently:

1. Fetches the full commit graph back to root (`FetchCommitGraph`, tree:0, no cutoff).
2. Walks first-parent to get a chain.
3. Plans checkpoints on that chain.

Nothing shares work across branches. For 152 feature branches that almost all descend from `main`, you're refetching and rewalking ~99% of main's history 152 times.

**Tier 1 — cheap: default-branch-first ordering.**

- Read `HEAD` symref from the source advertisement. Protocol v2 `ls-refs` returns it with the `symrefs` argument; v1 `info/refs` includes it in capabilities. git-sync already talks to the source at probe time, so no extra round-trip.
- Sort `planRefs` in bootstrap.go:465 so HEAD's branch is first; everything else stays in current order.
- Benefit at push time: the target's object store has trunk objects when feature-branch batches start, which means `receive-pack` handles subsequent batches as thin packs against a larger reachable set. This is free and small.

**Tier 2 — the real win: trunk-reachability cutoff.**

After planning trunk, you know every commit hash reachable from trunk's tip (you already compute the first-parent chain at bootstrap.go:581, and `FetchCommitGraph` materialized the full ancestor set). For every subsequent branch:

- Pass trunk tip as a `have` to `FetchCommitGraph`. Protocol v2 `fetch` honors haves; the source only sends commits not reachable from trunk. A "main + 5 commits" feature branch returns 5 commits instead of 75k.
- Short-circuit `FirstParentChain` when it hits a commit already in trunk's reachable set — no need to walk past the fork point. chain_len becomes divergence length, not absolute length.

Rough impact on your log output: today each branch takes ~150–400ms and chain_len is 40–128. Post-optimization, trunk is one ~normal planning pass, and each subsequent branch is probably ~20–50ms with chain_len in the single digits for the typical "branched off main last week" case. For 152 branches that's the difference between ~40s and ~5s on the planning phase alone, and the actual relay gets proportionally cheaper too because non-trunk batches shrink to their divergence deltas.

**Things to handle cleanly.**

- *No clear trunk.* If `HEAD` isn't advertised, or `--branch`/`--map` excludes it, fall back to current behavior. Alternatively pick the pivot as "branch with the longest graph" by doing one probe-time graph walk; probably not worth it — HEAD is the right heuristic 99% of the time.
- *Branches with disjoint roots.* The have-limited fetch just returns their full history; degrades gracefully.
- *Shallow planner* (bootstrap.go:602) already does incremental depth doubling per-branch; the same cross-branch reuse would apply there too — remember trunk's ancestry from the first full walk and stop shallow-depth doubling on other branches once they land inside it.
- *Cache lifecycle.* Trunk's reachable-set is the big thing to keep across branches. The current code explicitly nils `graphStore` and `runtime.GC()`s after extracting the chain (bootstrap.go:582) specifically because the full store is huge (4.6 GB for linux per the comment). You don't want to keep the full store around for 152 branches. But you only need the *hash set* of trunk's ancestry for cutoff purposes — that's 8-ish bytes per commit × ~75k commits = ~600 KB. Extract that set before you drop the store, carry it forward, drop it after planning finishes.

**Where in the code it goes.** The minimal version is: lift ordering into `planBatches` (bootstrap.go:489), compute trunk's reachability set on the first iteration, thread it as a cutoff into subsequent `FetchCommitGraph` calls and into `FirstParentChain`. That's two signature changes (`FetchCommitGraph` gains optional haves; `FirstParentChain` gains an optional "stop-at" set) and some wiring in the loop. No new concepts, just sharing what's already computed.

Worth a design note in docs/bootstrap-batching.md before implementing, since it changes the per-branch invariants the current code relies on. Want me to sketch that note?

41	// Params holds the inputs for a bootstrap execution.
42	type Params struct {
43		SourceConn    *gitproto.Conn
44		SourceService interface {
45			FetchPack(context.Context, *gitproto.Conn, map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) (io.ReadCloser, error)
46			FetchCommitGraph(context.Context, storer.Storer, *gitproto.Conn, gitproto.DesiredRef) error
47			SupportsBootstrapBatch() bool
48		}
49		TargetPusher interface {
50			PushPack(context.Context, []gitproto.PushCommand, io.ReadCloser) error
51			PushCommands(context.Context, []gitproto.PushCommand) error
52		}
53		DesiredRefs  map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef
54		TargetRefs   map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
55		MaxPackBytes int64

63		Plans             []planner.BranchPlan

65		Relay             bool

67		RelayReason       string
68		Batching          bool

76		chain []plumbing.Hash // full first-parent chain (root→tip) for subdividing on push failure
77	}
78	
79	// Execute runs the bootstrap strategy (one-shot or batched).
80	func Execute(ctx context.Context, p Params, relayReason string) (Result, error) {
81		if p.TargetPusher == nil {
82			return Result{Relay: true, RelayMode: "bootstrap", RelayReason: relayReason}, fmt.Errorf("bootstrap strategy requires TargetPusher")
83		}
84	
85		// GitHub large-repo preflight
86		if batchLimit, ok := githubBatchLimit(ctx, p); ok {
87			p.TargetMaxPack = batchLimit
88			p.log("bootstrap github preflight selected batched mode",
89				"target_max_pack_bytes", p.TargetMaxPack)

91	
92		planTargetRefs := p.TargetRefs
93		if p.TargetMaxPack > 0 {
94			planTargetRefs = adjustedBootstrapTargetRefs(p.DesiredRefs, p.TargetRefs)
95		}
96		plans, err := planner.BuildBootstrapPlans(p.DesiredRefs, planTargetRefs)
97		if err != nil {
98			return Result{}, err
99		}

90	// FetchCommitGraph fetches only the commit graph (tree:0 filter) for a ref.
91	// Requires v2 with filter support.
92	func (s *RefService) FetchCommitGraph(
93		ctx context.Context,
94		store storer.Storer,
95		conn *Conn,
96		ref DesiredRef,
97	) error {
98		if s.Protocol != "v2" {
99			return fmt.Errorf("commit graph fetch requires protocol v2")
100		}
101		if !s.V2Caps.FetchSupports("filter") {
102			return fmt.Errorf("source does not advertise fetch filter support")
103		}
104	
105		cmdArgs := []string{
106			"ofs-delta",
107			"no-progress",
108			"filter tree:0",
109			"want " + ref.SourceHash.String(),
110			"done",
111		}
112		body, err := EncodeCommand("fetch", s.V2Caps.RequestCapabilities(), cmdArgs)
113		if err != nil {
114			return err
115		}
116		reader, err := PostRPCStream(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, "upload-pack fetch")
117		if err != nil {
118			return err
119		}
120		defer ioutil.CheckClose(reader, &err)
121		// Commit-graph fetches are short and not user-facing; skip progress.
122		return storeV2FetchPack(store, reader, false)
123	}
124	
125	// Capabilities returns the sorted capability list for display.
126	func (s *RefService) Capabilities() []string {
127		switch s.Protocol {
128		case "v2":
129			return s.V2Caps.SortedKeys()
130		case "v1":
131			return AdvRefsCaps(s.V1Adv)
132		default:
133			return nil
134		}
135	}
136	
137	// --- V2 fetch implementation ---
138	
139	func fetchToStoreV2(
140		ctx context.Context,
141		store storer.Storer,
142		conn *Conn,
143		caps *V2Capabilities,
144		desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]DesiredRef,
145		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
146		verbose bool,
147	) error {
148		wants := collectWants(desired)
149		haves := SortedUniqueHashes(refValues(targetRefs))
150		if len(wants) == 0 {
151			return git.NoErrAlreadyUpToDate
152		}
153	
154		cmdArgs := make([]string, 0, len(wants)+len(haves)+4)
155		// NOTE: no "thin-pack" argument. The relayed pack must stay
156		// self-contained so callers (e.g. replicate) can forward it to
157		// receive-pack servers that may advertise "no-thin". See
158		// planner.SupportsReplicateRelay for the matching invariant.
159		cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "ofs-delta")
160		if !verbose {
161			cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "no-progress")
162		}
163		for _, h := range wants {
164			cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "want "+h.String())
165		}
166		for _, h := range haves {
167			cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "have "+h.String())
168		}
169		cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "done")
170	
171		body, err := EncodeCommand("fetch", caps.RequestCapabilities(), cmdArgs)
172		if err != nil {
173			return err
174		}
175		reader, err := PostRPCStream(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, "upload-pack fetch")
176		if err != nil {
177			return err
178		}
179		defer ioutil.CheckClose(reader, &err)
180		return storeV2FetchPack(store, reader, verbose)
181	}

1	package planner
2	
3	import (
4		"fmt"
5		"sort"
6	
7		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
8		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/object"
9		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/storer"
10	)
11	
12	// BootstrapBatch holds the checkpoint plan for a single branch during batched bootstrap.
13	type BootstrapBatch struct {
14		Plan        BranchPlan
15		TempRef     plumbing.ReferenceName
16		ResumeHash  plumbing.Hash
17		Checkpoints []plumbing.Hash
18	}
19	
20	// FirstParentChain walks the first-parent chain from tip back to root,
21	// returning the chain in root-to-tip order.
22	func FirstParentChain(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, tip plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {
23		commit, err := object.GetCommit(store, tip)
24		if err != nil {
25			return nil, err
26		}
27		chain := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, 128)
28		for {
29			chain = append(chain, commit.Hash)
30			if len(commit.ParentHashes) == 0 {
31				break
32			}
33			commit, err = object.GetCommit(store, commit.ParentHashes[0])
34			if err != nil {
35				return nil, err
36			}
37		}
38		// Reverse in-place to get root-to-tip order.
39		for i, j := 0, len(chain)-1; i < j; i, j = i+1, j-1 {
40			chain[i], chain[j] = chain[j], chain[i]
41		}
42		return chain, nil
43	}
44	
45	// FirstParentChainFromMap walks a first-parent map from tip back to root.
46	// The map key is a commit hash, the value is its first parent hash.
47	// A zero-value parent marks the root. Returns the chain in root-to-tip order.
48	func FirstParentChainFromMap(parents map[plumbing.Hash]plumbing.Hash, tip plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {
49		chain := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(parents))
50		current := tip
51		seen := make(map[plumbing.Hash]struct{}, len(parents))
52		for {
53			if _, ok := seen[current]; ok {
54				return nil, fmt.Errorf("cycle detected at %s", current)
55			}
56			seen[current] = struct{}{}
57			chain = append(chain, current)
58			parent, ok := parents[current]
59			if !ok {
60				return nil, fmt.Errorf("commit %s not found in parent map", current)
61			}
62			if parent.IsZero() {
63				break
64			}
65			current = parent
66		}
67		for i, j := 0, len(chain)-1; i < j; i, j = i+1, j-1 {
68			chain[i], chain[j] = chain[j], chain[i]
69		}
70		return chain, nil
71	}
72	
73	// SampledCheckpointCandidates generates a set of candidate indices to probe,
74	// sorted from largest (preferred) to smallest.
75	func SampledCheckpointCandidates(lo, hi int, prevSpan int) []int {
76		if lo > hi {
77			return nil
78		}
79		set := map[int]struct{}{}
80		add := func(idx int) {
81			if idx < lo {
82				idx = lo
83			}
84			if idx > hi {
85				idx = hi
86			}
87			set[idx] = struct{}{}
88		}
89	
90		projected := hi
91		if prevSpan > 0 {
92			projected = lo + prevSpan - 1
93		}
94		add(projected)
95	
96		const sampleCount = 4
97		current := projected
98		for i := 0; i < sampleCount-1; i++ {
99			if current <= lo {
100				add(lo)
101				continue
102			}
103			distance := current - lo
104			current = lo + distance/2
105			add(current)
106		}
107		add(lo)
108	
109		candidates := make([]int, 0, len(set))
110		for idx := range set {
111			candidates = append(candidates, idx)
112		}
113		sort.Sort(sort.Reverse(sort.IntSlice(candidates)))
114		return candidates
115	}
116	
117	// SampledCheckpointUnderLimit finds the largest checkpoint index that fits within
118	// the batch limit, using a sampling strategy to reduce probes (issue #14).
119	func SampledCheckpointUnderLimit(
120		chain []plumbing.Hash,
121		prevIdx int,
122		prevSpan int,
123		probe func(idx int) (tooLarge bool, err error),
124	) (int, error) {
125		lo := prevIdx + 1
126		hi := len(chain) - 1
127		if lo > hi {
128			return -1, nil
129		}
130	
131		samples := SampledCheckpointCandidates(lo, hi, prevSpan)
132		best := -1
133		for _, idx := range samples {
134			tooLarge, err := probe(idx)
135			if err != nil {
136				return -1, err
137			}
138			if tooLarge {
139				continue
140			}
141			best = idx
142			break
143		}
144		if best != -1 {
145			return best, nil
146		}
147	
148		if prevSpan > 1 {
149			shrunk := prevSpan / 2
150			if shrunk < 1 {
151				shrunk = 1
152			}
153			idx := lo + shrunk - 1
154			if idx > hi {
155				idx = hi
156			}
157			if idx >= lo {
158				tooLarge, err := probe(idx)
159				if err != nil {
160					return -1, err
161				}
162				if !tooLarge {
163					return idx, nil
164				}
165			}
166		}
167	
168		tooLarge, err := probe(lo)
169		if err != nil {
170			return -1, err
171		}
172		if tooLarge {
173			return -1, nil
174		}
175		return lo, nil
176	}
177	
178	// BootstrapResumeIndex finds the starting index in a checkpoint list given a resume hash.
179	func BootstrapResumeIndex(checkpoints []plumbing.Hash, resumeHash plumbing.Hash) (int, error) {
180		if resumeHash.IsZero() {
181			return 0, nil
182		}
183		for idx, checkpoint := range checkpoints {
184			if checkpoint == resumeHash {
185				return idx + 1, nil
186			}
187		}
188		return 0, fmt.Errorf("temp ref hash %s does not match any planned checkpoint", resumeHash)
189	}
190

1	package gitproto
2	
3	import (
4		"bufio"
5		"bytes"
6		"context"
7		"errors"
8		"fmt"
9		"io"
10		"strings"
11	
12		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
13		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/format/pktline"
14		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/protocol/packp"
15		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/transport"
16	)
17	
18	// RefService encapsulates the result of source ref discovery and the negotiated
19	// protocol, providing methods for subsequent fetch and pack operations.
20	type RefService struct {
21		Protocol string // "v1" or "v2"
22		V1Adv    *packp.AdvRefs
23		V2Caps   *V2Capabilities
24		// Verbose, when true, streams source-side sideband progress ("Counting
25		// objects", "Compressing objects", ...) to stderr and asks the source
26		// upload-pack to emit progress by not sending the no-progress option.
27		Verbose bool
28	}
29	
30	// ListSourceRefs discovers refs from the source using the configured protocol mode.
31	// Returns the list of refs and a RefService for subsequent operations.
32	func ListSourceRefs(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, protocolMode string, refPrefixes []string) ([]*plumbing.Reference, *RefService, error) {
33		switch protocolMode {
34		case "v1":
35			adv, refs, err := listSourceRefsV1(ctx, conn)
36			if err != nil {
37				return nil, nil, err
38			}
39			return refs, &RefService{Protocol: "v1", V1Adv: adv}, nil
40	
41		case "auto", "v2":
42			data, err := RequestInfoRefs(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, "version=2")
43			if err != nil {
44				return nil, nil, err
45			}
46			if caps, err := DecodeV2Capabilities(bytes.NewReader(data)); err == nil {
47				if !caps.Supports("ls-refs") || !caps.Supports("fetch") {
48					return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("source does not advertise required protocol v2 commands")
49				}
50				refs, err := listSourceRefsV2(ctx, conn, caps, refPrefixes)
51				if err != nil {
52					return nil, nil, err
53				}
54				return refs, &RefService{Protocol: "v2", V2Caps: caps}, nil
55			}
56			if protocolMode == "v2" {
57				return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("source did not negotiate protocol v2")
58			}
59			// Fall back to v1
60			adv, err := decodeV1AdvRefs(data)
61			if err != nil {
62				return nil, nil, err
63			}
64			refs, err := AdvRefsToSlice(adv)
65			if err != nil {
66				return nil, nil, err
67			}
68			return refs, &RefService{Protocol: "v1", V1Adv: adv}, nil
69	
70		default:
71			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("unsupported protocol mode %q", protocolMode)
72		}
73	}
74	
75	// AdvertisedRefsV1 fetches and decodes v1 advertised refs for the given service.
76	func AdvertisedRefsV1(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, service transport.Service) (*packp.AdvRefs, error) {
77		data, err := RequestInfoRefs(ctx, conn, service, "")
78		if err != nil {
79			return nil, err
80		}
81		return decodeV1AdvRefs(data)
82	}
83	
84	// AdvRefsToSlice converts an AdvRefs to a slice of references.
85	func AdvRefsToSlice(ar *packp.AdvRefs) ([]*plumbing.Reference, error) {
86		refs, err := ar.AllReferences()
87		if err != nil {
88			return nil, err
89		}
90		iter, err := refs.IterReferences()
91		if err != nil {
92			return nil, err
93		}
94		defer iter.Close()
95	
96		var out []*plumbing.Reference
97		err = iter.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
98			out = append(out, ref)
99			return nil
100		})
101		return out, err
102	}
103	
104	// AdvRefsCaps returns the sorted capability list from an AdvRefs.
105	func AdvRefsCaps(adv *packp.AdvRefs) []string {
106		if adv == nil || adv.Capabilities == nil {
107			return nil
108		}
109		all := adv.Capabilities.All()
110		items := make([]string, 0, len(all))
111		for _, cap := range all {
112			values := adv.Capabilities.Get(cap)
113			if len(values) == 0 {
114				items = append(items, string(cap))
115				continue
116			}
117			for _, value := range values {
118				items = append(items, string(cap)+"="+value)
119			}
120		}
121		return items
122	}
123	
124	func listSourceRefsV1(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn) (*packp.AdvRefs, []*plumbing.Reference, error) {
125		adv, err := AdvertisedRefsV1(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService)
126		if err != nil {
127			return nil, nil, err
128		}
129		refs, err := AdvRefsToSlice(adv)
130		if err != nil {
131			return nil, nil, err
132		}
133		return adv, refs, nil
134	}
135	
136	func listSourceRefsV2(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, caps *V2Capabilities, prefixes []string) ([]*plumbing.Reference, error) {
137		args := []string{"peel"}
138		for _, prefix := range prefixes {
139			args = append(args, "ref-prefix "+prefix)
140		}
141		body, err := EncodeCommand("ls-refs", caps.RequestCapabilities(), args)
142		if err != nil {
143			return nil, err
144		}
145		data, err := PostRPC(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, "upload-pack ls-refs")
146		if err != nil {
147			return nil, err
148		}
149		return decodeV2LSRefs(bytes.NewReader(data))
150	}
151	
152	func decodeV2LSRefs(r *bytes.Reader) ([]*plumbing.Reference, error) {
153		reader := NewPacketReader(r)
154		var refs []*plumbing.Reference
155		for {
156			kind, payload, err := reader.ReadPacket()
157			if err != nil {
158				return nil, err
159			}
160			if kind == PacketFlush {
161				return refs, nil
162			}
163			if kind != PacketData {
164				return nil, fmt.Errorf("unexpected packet type %v in ls-refs response", kind)
165			}
166			fields := strings.Fields(strings.TrimSpace(string(payload)))
167			if len(fields) < 2 {
168				return nil, fmt.Errorf("malformed ls-refs response line %q", payload)
169			}
170			hash := plumbing.NewHash(fields[0])
171			name := plumbing.ReferenceName(fields[1])
172			refs = append(refs, plumbing.NewHashReference(name, hash))
173		}
174	}
175	
176	func decodeV1AdvRefs(data []byte) (*packp.AdvRefs, error) {
177		rd := bufio.NewReader(bytes.NewReader(data))
178		consumedSmartHeader, err := consumeSmartInfoRefsHeader(rd)
179		if err != nil {
180			return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w; body-prefix=%q", err, bodyPreview(data))
181		}
182		if consumedSmartHeader {
183			if _, err := rd.Peek(1); errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {
184				return nil, transport.ErrEmptyRemoteRepository
185			}
186		}
187	
188		ar := packp.NewAdvRefs()
189		if err := ar.Decode(rd); err != nil {
190			if err == packp.ErrEmptyAdvRefs {
191				return nil, transport.ErrEmptyRemoteRepository
192			}
193			return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w; body-prefix=%q", err, bodyPreview(data))
194		}
195		return ar, nil
196	}
197	
198	func consumeSmartInfoRefsHeader(rd *bufio.Reader) (bool, error) {
199		_, prefix, err := pktline.PeekLine(rd)
200		if err != nil {
201			return false, err
202		}
203		if !bytes.HasPrefix(prefix, []byte("# service=")) {
204			return false, nil
205		}
206	
207		var reply packp.SmartReply
208		if err := reply.Decode(rd); err != nil {
209			return true, err
210		}
211		if reply.Service == "" {
212			return true, errors.New("missing smart HTTP service name")
213		}
214		return true, nil
215	}
216	
217	func bodyPreview(data []byte) string {
218		if len(data) == 0 {
219			return ""
220		}
221		limit := 200
222		if len(data) < limit {
223			limit = len(data)
224		}
225		preview := string(data[:limit])
226		preview = strings.ReplaceAll(preview, "\n", `\n`)
227		preview = strings.ReplaceAll(preview, "\r", `\r`)
228		if len(data) > limit {
229			preview += "..."
230		}
231		return preview
232	}
233	
234	// RefHashMap converts a reference slice to a map of name→hash.
235	func RefHashMap(refs []*plumbing.Reference) map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash {
236		out := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash, len(refs))
237		for _, ref := range refs {
238			if ref.Type() == plumbing.HashReference {
239				out[ref.Name()] = ref.Hash()
240			}
241		}
242		return out
243	}
244

323	// --- Session setup (issue #12) ---
324	
325	// syncSession holds the shared state for a sync operation, reducing
326	// setup duplication across Run, Bootstrap, Probe, and Fetch.
327	type syncSession struct {
328		cfg             Config
329		stats           *statsCollector
330		logger          *slog.Logger
331		sourceConn      *gitproto.Conn
332		sourceService   *gitproto.RefService
333		sourceRefMap    map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
334		target          *targetSession

336	}
337	
338	type targetSession struct {
339		conn     *gitproto.Conn
340		adv      *packp.AdvRefs
341		refMap   map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
342		features gitproto.TargetFeatures
343		policy   planner.RelayTargetPolicy
344		pusher   gitproto.Pusher
345	}
346	
347	// newSession performs the shared setup: protocol validation, mapping validation,
348	// connection creation, and ref discovery.
349	func newSession(ctx context.Context, cfg Config, needTarget bool) (*syncSession, error) {
350		mode, err := validation.NormalizeProtocolMode(cfg.ProtocolMode)
351		if err != nil {
352			return nil, err
353		}
354		cfg.ProtocolMode = mode
355		switch cfg.Mode {
356		case "", modeSync:
357			cfg.Mode = modeSync
358		case modeReplicate:
359		default:
360			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unsupported operation mode %q", cfg.Mode)
361		}
362		if _, err := validation.ValidateMappings(cfg.Mappings); err != nil {
363			return nil, err
364		}
365		if cfg.Mode == modeReplicate && cfg.Force {
366			return nil, fmt.Errorf("replicate does not support --force; use sync instead")
367		}
368	
369		s := &syncSession{
370			cfg:             cfg,
371			stats:           newStats(cfg.ShowStats),
372			measurementDone: startMeasurement(cfg.MeasureMemory),
373		}
374		if cfg.Verbose {
375			s.logger = slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(os.Stderr, &slog.HandlerOptions{
376				Level: slog.LevelInfo,
377			}))
378		}
379	
380		s.sourceConn, err = newConn(cfg.Source, "source", s.stats, cfg.HTTPClient)
381		if err != nil {
382			return nil, fmt.Errorf("create source transport: %w", err)
383		}
384	
385		refPrefixes := planner.RefPrefixes(cfg.Mappings, cfg.IncludeTags)
386		sourceRefs, sourceService, err := gitproto.ListSourceRefs(ctx, s.sourceConn, cfg.ProtocolMode, refPrefixes)
387		if err != nil {
388			return nil, fmt.Errorf("list source refs: %w", err)
389		}
390		sourceService.Verbose = cfg.Verbose
391		s.sourceService = sourceService
392		s.sourceRefMap = gitproto.RefHashMap(sourceRefs)
393	
394		if needTarget {
395			targetConn, err := newConn(cfg.Target, "target", s.stats, cfg.HTTPClient)
396			if err != nil {
397				return nil, fmt.Errorf("create target transport: %w", err)
398			}
399			targetAdv, err := gitproto.AdvertisedRefsV1(ctx, targetConn, transport.ReceivePackService)
400			if err != nil {
401				return nil, fmt.Errorf("list target refs: %w", err)
402			}
403			targetRefSlice, err := gitproto.AdvRefsToSlice(targetAdv)
404			if err != nil {
405				return nil, fmt.Errorf("decode target refs: %w", err)
406			}
407			targetRefMap := gitproto.RefHashMap(targetRefSlice)
408			targetFeatures := gitproto.TargetFeaturesFromAdvRefs(targetAdv)
409			s.target = &targetSession{
410				conn:     targetConn,
411				adv:      targetAdv,
412				refMap:   targetRefMap,
413				features: targetFeatures,
414				policy: planner.RelayTargetPolicy{
415					CapabilitiesKnown: targetFeatures.Known,
416					NoThin:            targetFeatures.NoThin,
417				},
418				pusher: gitproto.NewPusher(targetConn, targetAdv, cfg.Verbose),
419			}
420		}
421	
422		return s, nil
423	}

427	// Run executes a sync or plan operation.
428	func Run(ctx context.Context, cfg Config) (Result, error) {
429		s, err := newSession(ctx, cfg, true)
430		if err != nil {
431			return Result{}, err
432		}
433		if s.cfg.Mode == modeReplicate {
434			return s.runReplicate(ctx)
435		}
436		return s.runSync(ctx)
437	}

403	// --- Checkpoint planning ---
404	
405	func planBatches(ctx context.Context, p Params, desired []planner.DesiredRef) ([]plannedBatch, error) {
406		out := make([]plannedBatch, 0, len(desired))
407		for _, ref := range desired {
408			checkpoints, chain, err := planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx, p, ref)
409			if err != nil {
410				return nil, err
411			}
412			out = append(out, plannedBatch{
413				BootstrapBatch: planner.BootstrapBatch{
414					Plan: planner.BranchPlan{
415						Branch: ref.Label, SourceRef: ref.SourceRef,
416						TargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash,
417						Kind: ref.Kind, Action: planner.ActionCreate,
418					},
419					TempRef:     planner.BootstrapTempRef(ref.TargetRef),
420					ResumeHash:  p.TargetRefs[planner.BootstrapTempRef(ref.TargetRef)],
421					Checkpoints: checkpoints,
422				},
423				chain: chain,
424			})
425		}
426		return out, nil
427	}
428	
429	// PlanCheckpoints plans the checkpoint hashes for a single branch during batched bootstrap.
430	func PlanCheckpoints(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {
431		checkpoints, _, err := planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx, p, ref)
432		return checkpoints, err
433	}
434	
435	// estimatedBytesPerCommit is the heuristic for estimating pack size from commit
436	// count. Real repos range from ~5 KiB/commit (small web apps) to ~120 KiB
437	// (blob-heavy monorepos); most mature repos fall in 20–80 KiB. 64 KiB produces
438	// accurate batch counts for large repos (linux is ~66 KiB/commit) while
439	// slightly overestimating for small ones (harmless — extra batches finish fast).
440	// The PACK header pre-check and target-rejection retry catch remaining error.
441	const estimatedBytesPerCommit = 65536
442	
443	func planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, []plumbing.Hash, error) {
444		p.log("bootstrap batch fetching commit graph", "branch", ref.TargetRef.String())
445	
446		// Fetch all commits (tree:0 filter) into a temporary in-memory store.
447		// For linux this is ~1.4M commits at ~3.3 KiB each = ~4.6 GB transient.
448		// We extract the first-parent chain (~75k hashes, ~3 MB) immediately
449		// and discard the store so GC can reclaim the 4.6 GB. The transient
450		// spike is unavoidable with git protocol v2 (no first-parent-only
451		// fetch) and go-git's pack parser (needs full store for delta resolution).
452		graphStore := memory.NewStorage()
453		gpRef := gitproto.DesiredRef{SourceRef: ref.SourceRef, TargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash}
454		if err := p.SourceService.FetchCommitGraph(ctx, graphStore, p.SourceConn, gpRef); err != nil {
455			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("fetch bootstrap planning graph for %s: %w", ref.TargetRef, err)
456		}
457		chain, err := planner.FirstParentChain(graphStore, ref.SourceHash)
458		graphStore = nil // allow GC to reclaim the commit graph store (~4.6 GB for linux)
459		runtime.GC()
460		if err != nil {
461			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("walk first-parent chain for %s: %w", ref.TargetRef, err)
462		}
463		if len(chain) == 0 {
464			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("empty first-parent chain for %s", ref.TargetRef)
465		}
466	
467		numBatches := estimateBatchCount(int64(len(chain)), p.TargetMaxPack)
468		checkpoints := evenCheckpoints(chain, numBatches)
469	
470		p.log("bootstrap batch planned checkpoints",
471			"branch", ref.TargetRef.String(),
472			"chain_len", len(chain),
473			"estimated_batches", len(checkpoints))
474	
475		return checkpoints, chain, nil
476	}
477	
478	func estimateBatchCount(chainLen int64, batchMaxPack int64) int {
479		if batchMaxPack <= 0 || chainLen <= 0 {
480			return 1
481		}
482		estimated := chainLen * estimatedBytesPerCommit
483		n := int((estimated + batchMaxPack - 1) / batchMaxPack)
484		if n < 1 {
485			n = 1
486		}
487		return n
488	}
489	
490	// estimatedBytesPerObject is a conservative average for compressed git objects
491	// in a packfile. Used with the PACK header's object count to estimate total
492	// pack size before streaming the full pack. Real values range from ~200 bytes
493	// (tiny commits in a sparse repo) to ~2 KiB (blob-heavy repos), with most
494	// mature repos averaging 500–1000 bytes. 750 is a reasonable middle ground.
495	const estimatedBytesPerObject = 750
496	
497	// checkPackSizeAndSubdivide reads the 12-byte PACK header to get the object
498	// count, estimates total pack size, and if it exceeds batchLimit, closes the
499	// reader and calls subdivide(). Returns (nil, nil) when subdivided (caller
500	// should continue to retry), or (prepended reader, nil) to proceed with push.
501	func checkPackSizeAndSubdivide(
502		r io.ReadCloser,
503		batchLimit int64,
504		subdivide func() bool,
505	) (io.ReadCloser, error) {
506		var header [12]byte
507		n, err := io.ReadFull(r, header[:])
508		if err != nil {
509			// Short pack or error — let the push handle it

commit 3c20da59ca7924b317ab899d8eedc2f52f573f20
Author: Stefan Haubold <stefan@entire.io>
Date:   Tue Apr 14 16:23:09 2026 +0200

    Include completed branch tips as haves when fetching subsequent branches
    
    In a multi-branch bootstrap, each branch fetched as if the target was
    empty — the source had no way to know that previous branches had
    already pushed shared objects. For repos like linux where master and
    nocache-cleanup share ~99% of history, this meant re-transferring ~5 GiB
    of duplicate objects for the second branch.
    
    Fix: maintain a completedRefs map across the branch loop. After each
    branch finishes, add its tip (and temp ref) to the map. When fetching
    checkpoints for the next branch, merge completedRefs into the haves
    passed to FetchPack. The source can then delta-compress against objects
    the target already has, producing a much smaller pack for branches that
    share history.
    
    The tag phase already did this (it built tagTargetRefs from the
    completed batches). The branch-to-branch loop was the gap.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Entire-Checkpoint: da38c1b34ae1

23	// BuildDesiredRefs constructs the set of desired refs and managed targets from
24	// source refs and user configuration. All mapping validation happens here,
25	// before any network activity (issue #2, #3).
26	func BuildDesiredRefs(
27		sourceRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
28		cfg PlanConfig,
29	) (map[plumbing.ReferenceName]DesiredRef, map[plumbing.ReferenceName]ManagedTarget, error) {
30		desired := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]DesiredRef)
31		managed := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]ManagedTarget)
32	
33		addManaged := func(sourceRef, targetRef plumbing.ReferenceName, kind RefKind, hash plumbing.Hash) error {
34			if hash.IsZero() {
35				return fmt.Errorf("source ref %s not found", sourceRef)
36			}
37			// Reject duplicate target refs from different sources (issue #2, #3).
38			if existing, ok := desired[targetRef]; ok && existing.SourceRef != sourceRef {
39				return fmt.Errorf("duplicate target ref %s: mapped from both %s and %s", targetRef, existing.SourceRef, sourceRef)
40			}
41			short := targetRef.Short()
42			desired[targetRef] = DesiredRef{
43				Kind:       kind,
44				Label:      short,
45				SourceRef:  sourceRef,
46				TargetRef:  targetRef,
47				SourceHash: hash,
48			}
49			managed[targetRef] = ManagedTarget{Kind: kind, Label: short}
50			return nil
51		}
52	
53		if len(cfg.Mappings) > 0 {
54			// Validate all mappings up front (issue #2, #3)
55			normalized, err := validation.ValidateMappings(cfg.Mappings)
56			if err != nil {
57				return nil, nil, err
58			}
59			for _, nm := range normalized {
60				kind := RefKindFromName(nm.TargetRef)
61				if err := addManaged(nm.SourceRef, nm.TargetRef, kind, sourceRefs[nm.SourceRef]); err != nil {
62					return nil, nil, err
63				}
64			}
65		} else {
66			branches := BranchMapFromRefHashMap(sourceRefs)
67			selected := SelectBranches(branches, cfg.Branches)
68			for branch, hash := range selected {
69				refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branch)
70				if err := addManaged(refName, refName, RefKindBranch, hash); err != nil {
71					return nil, nil, err
72				}
73			}
74		}
75	
76		if cfg.IncludeTags {
77			for refName, hash := range sourceRefs {
78				if !refName.IsTag() {
79					continue
80				}

177	func (a *AdvRefs) addSymbolicRefs(s storer.ReferenceStorer) error {
178		for _, symref := range a.Capabilities.Get(capability.SymRef) {
179			chunks := strings.Split(symref, ":")
180			if len(chunks) != 2 {
181				err := fmt.Errorf("bad number of `:` in symref value (%q)", symref)
182				return err
183			}
184			name := plumbing.ReferenceName(chunks[0])
185			target := plumbing.ReferenceName(chunks[1])
186			ref := plumbing.NewSymbolicReference(name, target)
187			if err := s.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
188				return nil
189			}
190		}
191	
192		return nil
193	}

  "old_string": "\tswitch protocolMode {\n\tcase \"v1\":\n\t\tadv, refs, err := listSourceRefsV1(ctx, conn)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn refs, &RefService{Protocol: \"v1\", V1Adv: adv}, nil\n\n\tcase \"auto\", \"v2\":\n\t\tdata, err := RequestInfoRefs(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, \"version=2\")\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif caps, err := DecodeV2Capabilities(bytes.NewReader(data)); err == nil {\n\t\t\tif !caps.Supports(\"ls-refs\") || !caps.Supports(\"fetch\") {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"source does not advertise required protocol v2 commands\")\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\trefs, err := listSourceRefsV2(ctx, conn, caps, refPrefixes)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn refs, &RefService{Protocol: \"v2\", V2Caps: caps}, nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tif protocolMode == \"v2\" {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"source did not negotiate protocol v2\")\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Fall back to v1\n\t\tadv, err := decodeV1AdvRefs(data)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\trefs, err := AdvRefsToSlice(adv)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn refs, &RefService{Protocol: \"v1\", V1Adv: adv}, nil\n\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"unsupported protocol mode %q\", protocolMode)\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\tswitch protocolMode {\n\tcase \"v1\":\n\t\tadv, refs, err := listSourceRefsV1(ctx, conn)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn refs, &RefService{Protocol: \"v1\", V1Adv: adv, HeadTarget: headTargetFromAdv(adv)}, nil\n\n\tcase \"auto\", \"v2\":\n\t\tdata, err := RequestInfoRefs(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, \"version=2\")\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif caps, err := DecodeV2Capabilities(bytes.NewReader(data)); err == nil {\n\t\t\tif !caps.Supports(\"ls-refs\") || !caps.Supports(\"fetch\") {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"source does not advertise required protocol v2 commands\")\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\trefs, headTarget, err := listSourceRefsV2(ctx, conn, caps, refPrefixes)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn refs, &RefService{Protocol: \"v2\", V2Caps: caps, HeadTarget: headTarget}, nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tif protocolMode == \"v2\" {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"source did not negotiate protocol v2\")\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Fall back to v1\n\t\tadv, err := decodeV1AdvRefs(data)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\trefs, err := AdvRefsToSlice(adv)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn refs, &RefService{Protocol: \"v1\", V1Adv: adv, HeadTarget: headTargetFromAdv(adv)}, nil\n\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"unsupported protocol mode %q\", protocolMode)\n\t}"

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/gitproto/refs.go",
  "old_string": "func listSourceRefsV2(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, caps *V2Capabilities, prefixes []string) ([]*plumbing.Reference, error) {\n\targs := []string{\"peel\"}\n\tfor _, prefix := range prefixes {\n\t\targs = append(args, \"ref-prefix \"+prefix)\n\t}\n\tbody, err := EncodeCommand(\"ls-refs\", caps.RequestCapabilities(), args)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdata, err := PostRPC(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, \"upload-pack ls-refs\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\treturn decodeV2LSRefs(bytes.NewReader(data))\n}\n\nfunc decodeV2LSRefs(r *bytes.Reader) ([]*plumbing.Reference, error) {\n\treader := NewPacketReader(r)\n\tvar refs []*plumbing.Reference\n\tfor {\n\t\tkind, payload, err := reader.ReadPacket()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif kind == PacketFlush {\n\t\t\treturn refs, nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tif kind != PacketData {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"unexpected packet type %v in ls-refs response\", kind)\n\t\t}\n\t\tfields := strings.Fields(strings.TrimSpace(string(payload)))\n\t\tif len(fields) < 2 {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"malformed ls-refs response line %q\", payload)\n\t\t}\n\t\thash := plumbing.NewHash(fields[0])\n\t\tname := plumbing.ReferenceName(fields[1])\n\t\trefs = append(refs, plumbing.NewHashReference(name, hash))\n\t}\n}",
  "new_string": "func listSourceRefsV2(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, caps *V2Capabilities, prefixes []string) ([]*plumbing.Reference, plumbing.ReferenceName, error) {\n\targs := []string{\"peel\", \"symrefs\"}\n\tfor _, prefix := range prefixes {\n\t\targs = append(args, \"ref-prefix \"+prefix)\n\t}\n\tbody, err := EncodeCommand(\"ls-refs\", caps.RequestCapabilities(), args)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, \"\", err\n\t}\n\tdata, err := PostRPC(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, \"upload-pack ls-refs\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, \"\", err\n\t}\n\treturn decodeV2LSRefs(bytes.NewReader(data))\n}\n\nfunc decodeV2LSRefs(r *bytes.Reader) ([]*plumbing.Reference, plumbing.ReferenceName, error) {\n\treader := NewPacketReader(r)\n\tvar refs []*plumbing.Reference\n\tvar headTarget plumbing.ReferenceName\n\tfor {\n\t\tkind, payload, err := reader.ReadPacket()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, \"\", err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif kind == PacketFlush {\n\t\t\treturn refs, headTarget, nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tif kind != PacketData {\n\t\t\treturn nil, \"\", fmt.Errorf(\"unexpected packet type %v in ls-refs response\", kind)\n\t\t}\n\t\tfields := strings.Fields(strings.TrimSpace(string(payload)))\n\t\tif len(fields) < 2 {\n\t\t\treturn nil, \"\", fmt.Errorf(\"malformed ls-refs response line %q\", payload)\n\t\t}\n\t\thash := plumbing.NewHash(fields[0])\n\t\tname := plumbing.ReferenceName(fields[1])\n\t\trefs = append(refs, plumbing.NewHashReference(name, hash))\n\t\tif name == plumbing.HEAD {\n\t\t\tfor _, attr := range fields[2:] {\n\t\t\t\tif target, ok := strings.CutPrefix(attr, \"symref-target:\"); ok {\n\t\t\t\t\theadTarget = plumbing.ReferenceName(target)\n\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// headTargetFromAdv extracts the branch HEAD points to from v1 advertised\n// capabilities. Returns empty when HEAD is detached or no symref is advertised.\nfunc headTargetFromAdv(adv *packp.AdvRefs) plumbing.ReferenceName {\n\tif adv == nil || adv.Capabilities == nil {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\tfor _, value := range adv.Capabilities.Get(capability.SymRef) {\n\t\tparts := strings.SplitN(value, \":\", 2)\n\t\tif len(parts) != 2 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif plumbing.ReferenceName(parts[0]) == plumbing.HEAD {\n\t\t\treturn plumbing.ReferenceName(parts[1])\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn \"\"\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/gitproto/fetch.go",
  "old_string": "// FetchCommitGraph fetches only the commit graph (tree:0 filter) for a ref.\n// Requires v2 with filter support.\nfunc (s *RefService) FetchCommitGraph(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tstore storer.Storer,\n\tconn *Conn,\n\tref DesiredRef,\n) error {\n\tif s.Protocol != \"v2\" {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"commit graph fetch requires protocol v2\")\n\t}\n\tif !s.V2Caps.FetchSupports(\"filter\") {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"source does not advertise fetch filter support\")\n\t}\n\n\tcmdArgs := []string{\n\t\t\"ofs-delta\",\n\t\t\"no-progress\",\n\t\t\"filter tree:0\",\n\t\t\"want \" + ref.SourceHash.String(),\n\t\t\"done\",\n\t}\n\tbody, err := EncodeCommand(\"fetch\", s.V2Caps.RequestCapabilities(), cmdArgs)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\treader, err := PostRPCStream(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, \"upload-pack fetch\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tdefer ioutil.CheckClose(reader, &err)\n\t// Commit-graph fetches are short and not user-facing; skip progress.\n\treturn storeV2FetchPack(store, reader, false)\n}",
  "new_string": "// FetchCommitGraph fetches only the commit graph (tree:0 filter) for a ref.\n// Requires v2 with filter support. Optional haves let the source skip commits\n// already reachable from those hashes, which is valuable when planning later\n// branches that share history with an already-planned trunk.\nfunc (s *RefService) FetchCommitGraph(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tstore storer.Storer,\n\tconn *Conn,\n\tref DesiredRef,\n\thaves []plumbing.Hash,\n) error {\n\tif s.Protocol != \"v2\" {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"commit graph fetch requires protocol v2\")\n\t}\n\tif !s.V2Caps.FetchSupports(\"filter\") {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"source does not advertise fetch filter support\")\n\t}\n\n\tsortedHaves := SortedUniqueHashes(haves)\n\tcmdArgs := make([]string, 0, 4+len(sortedHaves))\n\tcmdArgs = append(cmdArgs,\n\t\t\"ofs-delta\",\n\t\t\"no-progress\",\n\t\t\"filter tree:0\",\n\t\t\"want \"+ref.SourceHash.String(),\n\t)\n\tfor _, h := range sortedHaves {\n\t\tcmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, \"have \"+h.String())\n\t}\n\tcmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, \"done\")\n\n\tbody, err := EncodeCommand(\"fetch\", s.V2Caps.RequestCapabilities(), cmdArgs)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\treader, err := PostRPCStream(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, \"upload-pack fetch\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tdefer ioutil.CheckClose(reader, &err)\n\t// Commit-graph fetches are short and not user-facing; skip progress.\n\treturn storeV2FetchPack(store, reader, false)\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/planner/checkpoint.go",
  "old_string": "// FirstParentChain walks the first-parent chain from tip back to root,\n// returning the chain in root-to-tip order.\nfunc FirstParentChain(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, tip plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\tcommit, err := object.GetCommit(store, tip)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tchain := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, 128)\n\tfor {\n\t\tchain = append(chain, commit.Hash)\n\t\tif len(commit.ParentHashes) == 0 {\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t\tcommit, err = object.GetCommit(store, commit.ParentHashes[0])\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// Reverse in-place to get root-to-tip order.\n\tfor i, j := 0, len(chain)-1; i < j; i, j = i+1, j-1 {\n\t\tchain[i], chain[j] = chain[j], chain[i]\n\t}\n\treturn chain, nil\n}",
  "new_string": "// FirstParentChain walks the first-parent chain from tip back to root,\n// returning the chain in root-to-tip order.\nfunc FirstParentChain(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, tip plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\treturn FirstParentChainStoppingAt(store, tip, nil)\n}\n\n// FirstParentChainStoppingAt walks the first-parent chain from tip back to\n// root, stopping early when a commit's hash is in stopAt. Returns the chain\n// in root-to-tip order, excluding any stopAt commits. When tip itself is in\n// stopAt, the returned chain is empty. A nil stopAt behaves like the plain\n// FirstParentChain walk.\n//\n// This supports trunk-aware planning: once trunk's ancestry is known, other\n// branches only need their divergence chain.\nfunc FirstParentChainStoppingAt(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, tip plumbing.Hash, stopAt map[plumbing.Hash]struct{}) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\tif _, stop := stopAt[tip]; stop {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\tcommit, err := object.GetCommit(store, tip)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tchain := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, 128)\n\tfor {\n\t\tchain = append(chain, commit.Hash)\n\t\tif len(commit.ParentHashes) == 0 {\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t\tparent := commit.ParentHashes[0]\n\t\tif _, stop := stopAt[parent]; stop {\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t\tcommit, err = object.GetCommit(store, parent)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// Reverse in-place to get root-to-tip order.\n\tfor i, j := 0, len(chain)-1; i < j; i, j = i+1, j-1 {\n\t\tchain[i], chain[j] = chain[j], chain[i]\n\t}\n\treturn chain, nil\n}"
}

275	// IterEncodedObjects returns an iterator for all objects of the given type.
276	func (o *ObjectStorage) IterEncodedObjects(t plumbing.ObjectType) (storer.EncodedObjectIter, error) {
277		var series []plumbing.EncodedObject
278		switch t {
279		case plumbing.AnyObject:
280			series = flattenObjectMap(o.Objects)
281		case plumbing.CommitObject:
282			series = flattenObjectMap(o.Commits)
283		case plumbing.TreeObject:
284			series = flattenObjectMap(o.Trees)
285		case plumbing.BlobObject:
286			series = flattenObjectMap(o.Blobs)
287		case plumbing.TagObject:
288			series = flattenObjectMap(o.Tags)
289		}
290	
291		return storer.NewEncodedObjectSliceIter(series), nil
292	}
293	
294	func flattenObjectMap(m map[plumbing.Hash]plumbing.EncodedObject) []plumbing.EncodedObject {
295		objects := make([]plumbing.EncodedObject, 0, len(m))
296		for _, obj := range m {
297			objects = append(objects, obj)
298		}
299		return objects
