41	// Params holds the inputs for a bootstrap execution.
42	type Params struct {
43		SourceConn    *gitproto.Conn
44		SourceService interface {
45			FetchPack(context.Context, *gitproto.Conn, map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) (io.ReadCloser, error)
46			FetchCommitGraph(context.Context, storer.Storer, *gitproto.Conn, gitproto.DesiredRef) error
47			SupportsBootstrapBatch() bool
48		}
49		TargetPusher interface {
50			PushPack(context.Context, []gitproto.PushCommand, io.ReadCloser) error
51			PushCommands(context.Context, []gitproto.PushCommand) error
52		}
53		DesiredRefs  map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef
54		TargetRefs   map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
55		MaxPackBytes int64
56		TargetMaxPack int64
57		Verbose      bool
58		Logger       *slog.Logger
59	}

61	// Result holds the outcome of the bootstrap strategy.
62	type Result struct {
63		Plans             []planner.BranchPlan
64		Pushed            int
65		Relay             bool
66		RelayMode         string
67		RelayReason       string
68		Batching          bool
69		BatchCount        int
70		PlannedBatchCount int
71		TempRefs          []string
72	}
73	
74	type plannedBatch struct {
75		planner.BootstrapBatch
76		chain []plumbing.Hash // full first-parent chain (root→tip) for subdividing on push failure
77	}
78	
79	// Execute runs the bootstrap strategy (one-shot or batched).
80	func Execute(ctx context.Context, p Params, relayReason string) (Result, error) {
81		if p.TargetPusher == nil {
82			return Result{Relay: true, RelayMode: "bootstrap", RelayReason: relayReason}, fmt.Errorf("bootstrap strategy requires TargetPusher")
83		}
84	
85		// GitHub large-repo preflight
86		if batchLimit, ok := githubBatchLimit(ctx, p); ok {
87			p.TargetMaxPack = batchLimit
88			p.log("bootstrap github preflight selected batched mode",
89				"target_max_pack_bytes", p.TargetMaxPack)
90		}
91	
92		planTargetRefs := p.TargetRefs
93		if p.TargetMaxPack > 0 {
94			planTargetRefs = adjustedBootstrapTargetRefs(p.DesiredRefs, p.TargetRefs)
95		}
96		plans, err := planner.BuildBootstrapPlans(p.DesiredRefs, planTargetRefs)
97		if err != nil {
98			return Result{}, err
99		}
100	
101		result := Result{
102			Plans: plans, Relay: true, RelayMode: "bootstrap", RelayReason: relayReason,
103		}
104	
105		if p.TargetMaxPack > 0 {
106			return executeBatched(ctx, p, plans, result)
107		}
108	
109		// One-shot bootstrap
110		p.log("bootstrap fetching refs from source", "ref_count", len(plans))
111		gpDesired := convert.DesiredRefs(p.DesiredRefs)
112		packReader, err := p.SourceService.FetchPack(ctx, p.SourceConn, gpDesired, nil)
113		if err != nil {

  "old_string": "// --- Checkpoint planning ---\n\nfunc planBatches(ctx context.Context, p Params, desired []planner.DesiredRef) ([]plannedBatch, error) {\n\tout := make([]plannedBatch, 0, len(desired))\n\tfor _, ref := range desired {\n\t\tcheckpoints, chain, err := planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx, p, ref)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tout = append(out, plannedBatch{\n\t\t\tBootstrapBatch: planner.BootstrapBatch{\n\t\t\t\tPlan: planner.BranchPlan{\n\t\t\t\t\tBranch: ref.Label, SourceRef: ref.SourceRef,\n\t\t\t\t\tTargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash,\n\t\t\t\t\tKind: ref.Kind, Action: planner.ActionCreate,\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t\tTempRef:     planner.BootstrapTempRef(ref.TargetRef),\n\t\t\t\tResumeHash:  p.TargetRefs[planner.BootstrapTempRef(ref.TargetRef)],\n\t\t\t\tCheckpoints: checkpoints,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\tchain: chain,\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\treturn out, nil\n}\n\n// PlanCheckpoints plans the checkpoint hashes for a single branch during batched bootstrap.\nfunc PlanCheckpoints(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\tcheckpoints, _, err := planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx, p, ref)\n\treturn checkpoints, err\n}",
  "new_string": "// --- Checkpoint planning ---\n\nfunc planBatches(ctx context.Context, p Params, desired []planner.DesiredRef) ([]plannedBatch, error) {\n\tordered, trunkIdx := orderTrunkFirst(desired, p.SourceHeadTarget)\n\tout := make([]plannedBatch, 0, len(ordered))\n\n\tvar (\n\t\ttrunkStopSet map[plumbing.Hash]struct{}\n\t\ttrunkHaves   []plumbing.Hash\n\t)\n\n\tfor i, ref := range ordered {\n\t\tvar (\n\t\t\thaves  []plumbing.Hash\n\t\t\tstopAt map[plumbing.Hash]struct{}\n\t\t)\n\t\tif i != trunkIdx && trunkStopSet != nil {\n\t\t\thaves = trunkHaves\n\t\t\tstopAt = trunkStopSet\n\t\t}\n\t\tcheckpoints, chain, ancestors, err := planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx, p, ref, haves, stopAt)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif i == trunkIdx && trunkIdx >= 0 {\n\t\t\ttrunkStopSet = ancestors\n\t\t\ttrunkHaves = []plumbing.Hash{ref.SourceHash}\n\t\t}\n\t\tout = append(out, plannedBatch{\n\t\t\tBootstrapBatch: planner.BootstrapBatch{\n\t\t\t\tPlan: planner.BranchPlan{\n\t\t\t\t\tBranch: ref.Label, SourceRef: ref.SourceRef,\n\t\t\t\t\tTargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash,\n\t\t\t\t\tKind: ref.Kind, Action: planner.ActionCreate,\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t\tTempRef:     planner.BootstrapTempRef(ref.TargetRef),\n\t\t\t\tResumeHash:  p.TargetRefs[planner.BootstrapTempRef(ref.TargetRef)],\n\t\t\t\tCheckpoints: checkpoints,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\tchain: chain,\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\treturn out, nil\n}\n\n// orderTrunkFirst returns desired reordered so the branch matching\n// sourceHeadTarget is first. If no match, the original order is preserved and\n// trunkIdx is -1. Otherwise trunkIdx is 0 (the position of the trunk).\nfunc orderTrunkFirst(desired []planner.DesiredRef, sourceHeadTarget plumbing.ReferenceName) ([]planner.DesiredRef, int) {\n\tif sourceHeadTarget == \"\" {\n\t\treturn desired, -1\n\t}\n\tfor i, ref := range desired {\n\t\tif ref.SourceRef == sourceHeadTarget {\n\t\t\tif i == 0 {\n\t\t\t\treturn desired, 0\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treordered := make([]planner.DesiredRef, 0, len(desired))\n\t\t\treordered = append(reordered, ref)\n\t\t\treordered = append(reordered, desired[:i]...)\n\t\t\treordered = append(reordered, desired[i+1:]...)\n\t\t\treturn reordered, 0\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn desired, -1\n}\n\n// PlanCheckpoints plans the checkpoint hashes for a single branch during batched bootstrap.\nfunc PlanCheckpoints(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\tcheckpoints, _, _, err := planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx, p, ref, nil, nil)\n\treturn checkpoints, err\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/strategy/bootstrap/bootstrap.go",
  "old_string": "func planCheckpointsFromChain(ctx context.Context, p Params, ref planner.DesiredRef) ([]plumbing.Hash, []plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\tp.log(\"bootstrap batch fetching commit graph\", \"branch\", ref.TargetRef.String())\n\n\t// Fetch all commits (tree:0 filter) into a temporary in-memory store.\n\t// For linux this is ~1.4M commits at ~3.3 KiB each = ~4.6 GB transient.\n\t// We extract the first-parent chain (~75k hashes, ~3 MB) immediately\n\t// and discard the store so GC can reclaim the 4.6 GB. The transient\n\t// spike is unavoidable with git protocol v2 (no first-parent-only\n\t// fetch) and go-git's pack parser (needs full store for delta resolution).\n\tgraphStore := memory.NewStorage()\n\tgpRef := gitproto.DesiredRef{SourceRef: ref.SourceRef, TargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash}\n\tif err := p.SourceService.FetchCommitGraph(ctx, graphStore, p.SourceConn, gpRef); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"fetch bootstrap planning graph for %s: %w\", ref.TargetRef, err)\n\t}\n\tchain, err := planner.FirstParentChain(graphStore, ref.SourceHash)\n\tgraphStore = nil // allow GC to reclaim the commit graph store (~4.6 GB for linux)\n\truntime.GC()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"walk first-parent chain for %s: %w\", ref.TargetRef, err)\n\t}\n\tif len(chain) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"empty first-parent chain for %s\", ref.TargetRef)\n\t}\n\n\tnumBatches := estimateBatchCount(int64(len(chain)), p.TargetMaxPack)\n\tcheckpoints := evenCheckpoints(chain, numBatches)\n\n\tp.log(\"bootstrap batch planned checkpoints\",\n\t\t\"branch\", ref.TargetRef.String(),\n\t\t\"chain_len\", len(chain),\n\t\t\"estimated_batches\", len(checkpoints))\n\n\treturn checkpoints, chain, nil\n}",
  "new_string": "// planCheckpointsFromChain fetches the source-side commit graph for a branch\n// and derives checkpoint hashes for batched bootstrap. When trunkStopAt is\n// provided, the walk terminates at commits already reachable from the trunk,\n// and trunkHaves is passed to the source fetch so shared history is not\n// resent. The returned ancestors set covers every commit in the fetched graph\n// (not just the first-parent chain), so callers can accumulate it as a\n// cutoff for subsequent branches.\nfunc planCheckpointsFromChain(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tp Params,\n\tref planner.DesiredRef,\n\ttrunkHaves []plumbing.Hash,\n\ttrunkStopAt map[plumbing.Hash]struct{},\n) ([]plumbing.Hash, []plumbing.Hash, map[plumbing.Hash]struct{}, error) {\n\tp.log(\"bootstrap batch fetching commit graph\",\n\t\t\"branch\", ref.TargetRef.String(),\n\t\t\"have_count\", len(trunkHaves),\n\t\t\"stop_at_count\", len(trunkStopAt))\n\n\t// Fetch all commits (tree:0 filter) into a temporary in-memory store.\n\t// Without haves this is ~1.4M commits for linux (~4.6 GB transient).\n\t// With trunk haves, the source only sends commits not reachable from\n\t// trunk, which for typical feature branches is tiny. We extract the\n\t// first-parent chain immediately and discard the store so GC can\n\t// reclaim it. The transient spike is unavoidable with git protocol v2\n\t// (no first-parent-only fetch) and go-git's pack parser (needs full\n\t// store for delta resolution).\n\tgraphStore := memory.NewStorage()\n\tgpRef := gitproto.DesiredRef{SourceRef: ref.SourceRef, TargetRef: ref.TargetRef, SourceHash: ref.SourceHash}\n\tif err := p.SourceService.FetchCommitGraph(ctx, graphStore, p.SourceConn, gpRef, trunkHaves); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"fetch bootstrap planning graph for %s: %w\", ref.TargetRef, err)\n\t}\n\tchain, err := planner.FirstParentChainStoppingAt(graphStore, ref.SourceHash, trunkStopAt)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(\"walk first-parent chain for %s: %w\", ref.TargetRef, err)\n\t}\n\t// Collect all commit hashes in the fetched graph so callers can extend\n\t// their stop set. We only need the keys — ~8 bytes per commit, so the\n\t// linux ancestry set is ~11 MB vs the store's ~4.6 GB.\n\tancestors := collectCommitHashes(graphStore)\n\tgraphStore = nil // allow GC to reclaim the commit graph store\n\truntime.GC()\n\n\tif len(chain) == 0 {\n\t\t// Tip is already covered by the stop set. Emit a single-checkpoint\n\t\t// batch so downstream push logic still creates the target ref; the\n\t\t// accompanying fetch will find nothing new via haves.\n\t\tchain = []plumbing.Hash{ref.SourceHash}\n\t}\n\n\tnumBatches := estimateBatchCount(int64(len(chain)), p.TargetMaxPack)\n\tcheckpoints := evenCheckpoints(chain, numBatches)\n\n\tp.log(\"bootstrap batch planned checkpoints\",\n\t\t\"branch\", ref.TargetRef.String(),\n\t\t\"chain_len\", len(chain),\n\t\t\"estimated_batches\", len(checkpoints))\n\n\treturn checkpoints, chain, ancestors, nil\n}\n\n// collectCommitHashes returns the set of commit hashes in store. Used to build\n// the trunk reachability set for subsequent branches' stop-at walks.\nfunc collectCommitHashes(store *memory.Storage) map[plumbing.Hash]struct{} {\n\titer, err := store.IterEncodedObjects(plumbing.CommitObject)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tdefer iter.Close()\n\tout := map[plumbing.Hash]struct{}{}\n\t_ = iter.ForEach(func(obj plumbing.EncodedObject) error {\n\t\tout[obj.Hash()] = struct{}{}\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\treturn out\n}"
}

216	func TestDecodeV2LSRefs(t *testing.T) {
217		// Build a valid ls-refs response:
218		// Each line: "<hash> <refname>\n"
219		wire := "" +
220			FormatPktLine("aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa refs/heads/main\n") +
221			FormatPktLine("bbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbb refs/heads/dev\n") +
222			"0000" // flush
223	
224		refs, err := decodeV2LSRefs(bytes.NewReader([]byte(wire)))

226			t.Fatalf("decodeV2LSRefs: %v", err)
227		}
228		if len(refs) != 2 {
229			t.Fatalf("expected 2 refs, got %d", len(refs))
230		}
231		if refs[0].Name().String() != "refs/heads/main" {
232			t.Errorf("refs[0].Name() = %q, want %q", refs[0].Name(), "refs/heads/main")

234		if refs[0].Hash().String() != "aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa" {
235			t.Errorf("refs[0].Hash() = %q", refs[0].Hash())
236		}

238			t.Errorf("refs[1].Name() = %q, want %q", refs[1].Name(), "refs/heads/dev")
239		}
240	}

> AGENT

Good — only bootstrap uses commit-graph fetches, so the single plumbing change is enough.

Quick status:

**Implemented both tiers:**

- Tier 1 — default-branch-first: `orderTrunkFirst` in bootstrap.go uses the new `SourceHeadTarget` param (derived from the v2 `symrefs` response or v1 `symref=` capability) to sort the trunk first; the rest of the order is preserved.
- Tier 2 — trunk-reachability cutoff: `planCheckpointsFromChain` now takes `trunkHaves []plumbing.Hash` (passed as haves to `FetchCommitGraph`, so the source skips commits reachable from trunk) and `trunkStopAt map[plumbing.Hash]struct{}` (passed to the new `FirstParentChainStoppingAt`, so the walk terminates at trunk ancestors). The trunk's full commit set is extracted from the graph store via `collectCommitHashes` before the store is GC'd — ~8 B × commit count, so ~11 MB for linux vs the ~4.6 GB store.

**Files touched:**
- `internal/gitproto/refs.go` — HEAD symref extraction (v1 via `capability.SymRef`, v2 via `symrefs` ls-refs arg); new `RefService.HeadTarget` field.
- `internal/gitproto/fetch.go` — `FetchCommitGraph` takes a haves slice.
- `internal/planner/checkpoint.go` — added `FirstParentChainStoppingAt`, kept `FirstParentChain` as a thin wrapper.
- `internal/strategy/bootstrap/bootstrap.go` — new `SourceHeadTarget` param, `orderTrunkFirst`, trunk cutoff wiring, empty-chain fallback for subsumed branches.
- `internal/syncer/syncer.go` — plumbs `s.sourceService.HeadTarget` into `bstrap.Params`.
- Tests: `internal/planner/planner_test.go`, `internal/gitproto/refs_test.go`, `internal/gitproto/fetch_test.go`, `internal/strategy/bootstrap/bootstrap_test.go` — new unit tests for stop-set walk, `orderTrunkFirst` ordering edge cases (HEAD filtered out, no HEAD hint, non-zero trunk index), v1 HEAD symref parsing, and v2 ls-refs symref-target attribute parsing.

**Behavior when HEAD is unknown or filtered out by `--branch`:** falls back to existing per-branch behavior; no regression.

`go build ./...` and `go test ./...` both green. The gated e2e integration tests (`GITSYNC_E2E_GIT_HTTP_BACKEND=1`, `GITSYNC_E2E_LIVE_LINUX=1`) will exercise the new path end-to-end when run.

Want me to add a note to `docs/bootstrap-batching.md` describing this change before committing?

> AGENT

No — and that's exactly why you're seeing `stop_at_count=0` for every branch. There are two issues:

**Issue 1 — the actual bug: HEAD is being filtered out of the v2 ls-refs response.**

The v2 `ls-refs` call uses `ref-prefix refs/heads/` (from `planner.RefPrefixes` at internal/planner/types.go:159). `HEAD` doesn't start with `refs/heads/`, so it's excluded from the response entirely. My `symrefs` argument was correct but it never gets a chance to fire because HEAD isn't returned in the first place. Result: `HeadTarget` is always empty on v2 sources, `orderTrunkFirst` returns `trunkIdx=-1`, the rest of the optimization degrades silently to the old per-branch behavior. Your log exactly matches: planner starts alphabetically (`20260116-…`), all `have_count=0 stop_at_count=0`.

**Issue 2 — the observability gap: we don't log whether HEAD was detected.**

Even if HEAD had been found, we don't log the trunk choice. Silent optimization is the wrong shape here — if the fast path ever doesn't fire you have no way to notice.

Fixing both:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Ph721CCLWpRfyLUqu6Ld48
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/gitproto/refs.go",
  "old_string": "func listSourceRefsV2(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, caps *V2Capabilities, prefixes []string) ([]*plumbing.Reference, plumbing.ReferenceName, error) {\n\targs := []string{\"peel\", \"symrefs\"}\n\tfor _, prefix := range prefixes {\n\t\targs = append(args, \"ref-prefix \"+prefix)\n\t}\n\tbody, err := EncodeCommand(\"ls-refs\", caps.RequestCapabilities(), args)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, \"\", err\n\t}\n\tdata, err := PostRPC(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, \"upload-pack ls-refs\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, \"\", err\n\t}\n\treturn decodeV2LSRefs(bytes.NewReader(data))\n}",
  "new_string": "func listSourceRefsV2(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, caps *V2Capabilities, prefixes []string) ([]*plumbing.Reference, plumbing.ReferenceName, error) {\n\t// Always include \"HEAD\" so the server returns the symref-target attribute\n\t// for HEAD. Without this, callers that pass only \"refs/heads/\" or\n\t// \"refs/tags/\" prefixes filter HEAD out of the response and lose the\n\t// default-branch hint that bootstrap planning uses as a trunk cutoff.\n\targs := []string{\"peel\", \"symrefs\", \"ref-prefix HEAD\"}\n\tfor _, prefix := range prefixes {\n\t\targs = append(args, \"ref-prefix \"+prefix)\n\t}\n\tbody, err := EncodeCommand(\"ls-refs\", caps.RequestCapabilities(), args)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, \"\", err\n\t}\n\tdata, err := PostRPC(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, \"upload-pack ls-refs\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, \"\", err\n\t}\n\treturn decodeV2LSRefs(bytes.NewReader(data))\n}"
}
```

}

> AGENT

All green. Summary:

**Fix 1 — the silent bug.** `listSourceRefsV2` now always adds `ref-prefix HEAD` to the ls-refs args, and `decodeV2LSRefs` extracts the symref target from HEAD and then drops HEAD from the returned ref slice (matches v1 behavior via the symref capability). Previously, because `planner.RefPrefixes` always passes `refs/heads/`, HEAD was filtered out of the v2 response entirely and `HeadTarget` was always empty on v2 sources — exactly what your log showed.

**Fix 2 — observability.** `planBatches` now emits one of three log lines up front:

- `bootstrap batch trunk unset reason="source did not advertise a HEAD symref"` — source gave us nothing (rare but possible on minimal git servers).
- `bootstrap batch trunk unset reason="HEAD ref not in desired set (filtered by --branch or --map)" source_head_target=refs/heads/main` — source advertised HEAD but the user's scope excluded it.
- `bootstrap batch trunk selected source_head_target=refs/heads/main trunk_target_ref=refs/heads/main` — fast path active; the first branch in the checkpoint logs after this should show `have_count=1 stop_at_count=<trunk_ancestry>`.

Re-run your command and you should now see the trunk-selected line, then the first branch planning with `have_count=0 stop_at_count=0` (trunk itself), then subsequent branches with `have_count=1 stop_at_count=<big number>` and drastically shorter `chain_len` values.

> DEVELOPER

the old run did cause a lot of webhook triggers from github, which I guess is expected given we pushed each branch individually. Should this still be the case with the new approach?

> AGENT

Short answer: **yes, expect roughly the same webhook volume** — the optimization I implemented is entirely on the *planning* side, not the *push* side, and GitHub fires one webhook event per ref update regardless of how those pushes are batched.

Here's the precise picture:

**What the trunk-first change reduces**
- Source-side commit-graph fetches (smaller, faster, fewer objects).
- `FirstParentChain` walk length per branch.
- Objects actually transferred in later batch packs (because have-negotiation via completed branch tips, already in place since 3c20da5).

**What it does not reduce**
- Number of `receive-pack` invocations against the target.
- Number of ref updates.
- Number of webhook deliveries from GitHub.

The batched bootstrap's push pattern is still: for each branch, push its checkpoint pack(s) to `refs/gitsync/bootstrap/heads/<branch>`, then cut over to the real `refs/heads/<branch>`. With 152 branches that's still ~152 temp-ref updates plus ~152 cutover updates, and GitHub generates a push event per ref-update, not per HTTP call.

**Three levers you could actually pull to cut webhook volume**

1. **Disable the webhook on the target during bootstrap.** By far the easiest operationally: most teams do exactly this for migrations and large one-shot imports. Re-enable after the final cutover batch. No code changes needed.

2. **Skip the pack push for fully-subsumed branches.** My trunk-first change already produces `chain=[tip]` for branches whose tip lives inside trunk's ancestry. Today that still does a tiny fetch + push. We could detect this case (branch tip is in the trunk reachable set) and emit a single ref-creation command with no pack at all — that's still a webhook, but fewer HTTP calls and no spurious "0 objects" pushes. Small win.

3. **Batch cutover ref updates into a single `receive-pack` call.** `receive-pack` accepts N ref-update commands in one push. Doing one HTTP call with 152 cutover commands instead of 152 separate calls would cut HTTP overhead a lot — but GitHub still fires 152 push events, one per ref. So this helps throughput, not webhook noise.

If webhooks are the real pain point, option 1 is the right answer; the tooling can't do better than git's per-ref event model. If you want me to implement option 2 (skip-pack for subsumed branches) or add a `--disable-target-webhooks` helper that uses the GitHub API to toggle them around the run, say the word — both are reasonable follow-ups but not part of the current planning optimization.

> DEVELOPER

to do 2, we need the whole graph in memory?

> AGENT

No — we already have exactly what we need, and it's the same ~11 MB hash set the trunk-first optimization already keeps in memory.

Look at the current flow in bootstrap.go:

1. Fetch trunk's commit graph into `graphStore` (~4.6 GB transient for linux).
2. `collectCommitHashes(graphStore)` — extracts *just the hashes* into `ancestors` (~8 B × commit count ≈ 11 MB for linux).
3. Drop `graphStore`, GC reclaims the 4.6 GB.
4. `ancestors` becomes `trunkStopSet`, carried across the per-branch loop.

`trunkStopSet` already contains every commit reachable from the trunk tip (not just the first-parent chain — `collectCommitHashes` iterates all `CommitObject`s in the store, and the tree:0 fetch returns the full reachability). So "is this branch tip fully subsumed by trunk?" is a one-line O(1) check against a set we already have:

```go
if _, subsumed := trunkStopSet[ref.SourceHash]; subsumed {
    // skip the commit-graph fetch and the pack push entirely
    // emit a single ref-create command pointing at ref.SourceHash
    continue
}
```

That's it. No additional memory. In fact it makes things *cheaper* than the current code path, because today when a branch is subsumed my code still fetches its commit graph (which returns near-nothing via the have-negotiation but still round-trips) and does a tiny push. Option 2 would skip both.

The only genuine caveat — not a memory one — is correctness edge cases:

- **Branch tip == trunk tip**: trivially in the set, skip is safe. Webhook fires for the ref creation, but no pack overhead.
- **Branch is an older commit on trunk's first-parent line**: in the set, skip is safe.
- **Branch is reachable only via a non-first-parent merge on trunk**: still in the set (because `collectCommitHashes` pulls every commit the tree:0 fetch returned, including second parents and their ancestors), so skip is still safe.
- **Branch tip not reachable from trunk at all**: not in the set, falls through to the current planning path. Correct.

So the question is really about code surface, not memory. Want me to implement it as a follow-up to the current change?

> DEVELOPER

yes

145	func adjustedBootstrapTargetRefs(
146		desiredRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef,
147		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
148	) map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash {
149		if len(targetRefs) == 0 {
150			return targetRefs
151		}
152		adjusted := planner.CopyRefHashMap(targetRefs)
153		for targetRef, desired := range desiredRefs {
154			if desired.Kind != planner.RefKindBranch {
155				continue
156			}
157			tempRef := planner.BootstrapTempRef(targetRef)
158			if adjusted[targetRef] == desired.SourceHash && adjusted[tempRef] == desired.SourceHash {
159				adjusted[targetRef] = plumbing.ZeroHash
160			}
161		}
162		return adjusted
163	}

811	func makeLinearCommitChain(tb testing.TB, count int) []plumbing.Hash {
812		tb.Helper()
813		store := memory.NewStorage()
814		return writeLinearCommitChain(tb, store, count)
815	}
816	
817	func writeLinearCommitChain(tb testing.TB, store storer.Storer, count int) []plumbing.Hash {
818		tb.Helper()
819		hashes := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, count)
820		for i := 0; i < count; i++ {
821			obj := store.NewEncodedObject()
822			var parents []plumbing.Hash
823			if len(hashes) > 0 {
824				parents = []plumbing.Hash{hashes[len(hashes)-1]}
825			}
826			when := time.Unix(int64(i+1), 0).UTC()
827			commit := &object.Commit{
828				Author:       object.Signature{Name: "test", Email: "test@example.com", When: when},
829				Committer:    object.Signature{Name: "test", Email: "test@example.com", When: when},
830				Message:      fmt.Sprintf("commit-%d", i),
831				TreeHash:     plumbing.ZeroHash,
832				ParentHashes: parents,
833			}
834			if err := commit.Encode(obj); err != nil {
835				tb.Fatalf("encode commit %d: %v", i, err)
836			}
837			hash, err := store.SetEncodedObject(obj)
838			if err != nil {
839				tb.Fatalf("store commit %d: %v", i, err)
840			}
841			hashes = append(hashes, hash)
842		}
843		return hashes
844	}

230	// BuildBootstrapPlans creates plans for an empty-target bootstrap.
231	func BuildBootstrapPlans(
232		desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]DesiredRef,
233		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
234	) ([]BranchPlan, error) {
235		targetNames := make([]plumbing.ReferenceName, 0, len(desired))
236		for _, want := range desired {
237			targetNames = append(targetNames, want.TargetRef)
238		}
239		sort.Slice(targetNames, func(i, j int) bool { return targetNames[i] < targetNames[j] })
240	
241		plans := make([]BranchPlan, 0, len(targetNames))
242		for _, targetRef := range targetNames {
243			targetHash := targetRefs[targetRef]
244			if !targetHash.IsZero() {
245				return nil, fmt.Errorf("target ref %s already exists; use sync for non-bootstrap runs", targetRef)
246			}
247			want := desired[targetRef]
248			plans = append(plans, BranchPlan{
249				Branch:     want.Label,
250				SourceRef:  want.SourceRef,
251				TargetRef:  want.TargetRef,
252				SourceHash: want.SourceHash,
253				TargetHash: plumbing.ZeroHash,
254				Kind:       want.Kind,
255				Action:     ActionCreate,
256				Reason:     fmt.Sprintf("create %s at %s", want.TargetRef, ShortHash(want.SourceHash)),
257			})
258		}
259		return plans, nil
260	}
261	
262	// PlanRef determines the action for a single ref that exists on both source and target.
263	func PlanRef(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, want DesiredRef, targetHash plumbing.Hash, force bool) (BranchPlan, error) {
264		plan := BranchPlan{
265			Branch:     want.Label,
266			SourceRef:  want.SourceRef,
267			TargetRef:  want.TargetRef,
268			SourceHash: want.SourceHash,
269			TargetHash: targetHash,
270			Kind:       want.Kind,
271		}
272	
273		if want.SourceHash == targetHash {
274			plan.Action = ActionSkip
275			plan.Reason = fmt.Sprintf("%s already current", ShortHash(want.SourceHash))
276			return plan, nil
277		}
278	
279		if want.Kind == RefKindTag {
280			if force {
281				plan.Action = ActionUpdate
282				plan.Reason = fmt.Sprintf("%s -> %s (force tag update)", ShortHash(targetHash), ShortHash(want.SourceHash))
283				return plan, nil
284			}

subsumed branch should have been skipped\", ref.SourceRef)\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\twriteLinearCommitChain(t, store, 3)\n\t\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\tfetchPack: func(_ context.Context, _ *gitproto.Conn, desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, _ map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) (io.ReadCloser, error) {\n\t\t\t\tpackFetches++\n\t\t\t\tif _, ok := desired[featureRef]; ok {\n\t\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"unexpected pack fetch including feature ref: %+v\", desired)\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\treturn io.NopCloser(bytes.NewReader([]byte(\"PACK\"))), nil\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t},\n\t\tTargetPusher: fakeBootstrapPusher{\n\t\t\tpushPack: func(_ context.Context, _ []gitproto.PushCommand, pack io.ReadCloser) error {\n\t\t\t\tpushPackCalls++\n\t\t\t\t_ = pack.Close()\n\t\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\tpushCommands: func(_ context.Context, cmds []gitproto.PushCommand) error {\n\t\t\t\tpushCommandsBatches = append(pushCommandsBatches, append([]gitproto.PushCommand(nil), cmds...))\n\t\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t},\n\t\tDesiredRefs: map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef{\n\t\t\tmainRef:    {SourceRef: mainRef, TargetRef: mainRef, SourceHash: mainHash, Kind: planner.RefKindBranch, Label: \"main\"},\n\t\t\tfeatureRef: {SourceRef: featureRef, TargetRef: featureRef, SourceHash: featureHash, Kind: planner.RefKindBranch, Label: \"feature\"},\n\t\t},\n\t\tTargetRefs:       map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash{},\n\t\tSourceHeadTarget: mainRef,\n\t\tTargetMaxPack:    1024 * 1024,\n\t}, \"empty target\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Execute: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif graphFetches != 1 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"fetchCommitGraph called %d times, want 1 (trunk only)\", graphFetches)\n\t}\n\tif packFetches != 1 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"fetchPack called %d times, want 1 (trunk only)\", packFetches)\n\t}\n\tif pushPackCalls != 1 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"PushPack called %d times, want 1 (trunk only)\", pushPackCalls)\n\t}\n\n\tvar foundFeatureCreate bool\n\tfor _, cmds := range pushCommandsBatches {\n\t\tfor _, cmd := range cmds {\n\t\t\tif cmd.Name == featureRef && cmd.New == featureHash && cmd.Old == plumbing.ZeroHash && !cmd.Delete {\n\t\t\t\tfoundFeatureCreate = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif !foundFeatureCreate {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected ref-create command for feature at %s; got %v\", featureHash, pushCommandsBatches)\n\t}\n}\n\ntype fakeBootstrapSource struct {"
}

> AGENT

Done. Summary of what option 2 adds:

**Planning-side (`planBatches`)**: before each non-trunk branch's commit-graph fetch, check `trunkStopSet[ref.SourceHash]`. On hit, emit a `plannedBatch{subsumed: true}` with just a `BranchPlan` (no TempRef, no Checkpoints, no chain). Log line: `bootstrap batch branch subsumed by trunk branch=<ref> source_hash=<sha>`.

**Execution-side (`executeBatched`)**: at the top of the batch loop, branch on `batch.subsumed`. On hit: single `PushCommands` call with one `ref-create` at `SourceHash`, update `completedRefs`, increment `BatchCount`, log `bootstrap batch subsumed branch finalized`. No temp ref, no pack fetch, no pack push, no cutover-then-delete dance.

**Cost saved per subsumed branch**: one commit-graph fetch + one pack fetch + one pack push + one temp-ref-update + one cutover push + one temp-ref delete → replaced by a single ref-create command. On webhooks you still get one event for the ref creation; everything else is pure network/CPU savings.

**Correctness**: `trunkStopSet` is built from *every* `CommitObject` in trunk's fetched graph (not just first-parent), so any branch tip that's reachable through trunk's merge parents is also detected as subsumed. Non-subsumed branches flow through the unchanged original path. If there's no trunk (HEAD unavailable or filtered out), `trunkStopSet` is nil and the subsumed check is skipped entirely — no regression.

Test `TestExecuteBatchedSubsumedBranchSkipsPack` in bootstrap_test.go verifies: `fetchCommitGraph` is called once (trunk only), `fetchPack` is called once (trunk only), `PushPack` is called once (trunk only), and the feature branch's ref is created by a single `PushCommands` with `Old=0, New=featureHash, Delete=false`.

All tests green.

449	func TestExecuteBatchedSubsumedBranchSkipsPack(t *testing.T) {
450		mainRef := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName("main")
451		featureRef := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName("feature")
452		// Linear chain: hashes[0] -> hashes[1] -> hashes[2]. main tip = hashes[2],
453		// feature tip = hashes[0]. feature is entirely within main's ancestry, so
454		// trunk-first planning should mark it subsumed and emit zero pack pushes
455		// for it.
456		hashes := makeLinearCommitChain(t, 3)
457		mainHash := hashes[2]
458		featureHash := hashes[0]
459	
460		var (
461			graphFetches        int
462			packFetches         int
463			pushPackCalls       int
464			pushCommandsBatches [][]gitproto.PushCommand
465		)
466	
467		_, err := Execute(context.Background(), Params{
468			SourceService: fakeBootstrapSource{
469				fetchCommitGraph: func(_ context.Context, store storer.Storer, _ *gitproto.Conn, ref gitproto.DesiredRef, _ []plumbing.Hash) error {
470					graphFetches++
471					if ref.SourceRef != mainRef {
472						t.Errorf("unexpected commit-graph fetch for %s; subsumed branch should have been skipped", ref.SourceRef)
473					}
474					writeLinearCommitChain(t, store, 3)
475					return nil
476				},
477				fetchPack: func(_ context.Context, _ *gitproto.Conn, desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, _ map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) (io.ReadCloser, error) {
478					packFetches++
479					if _, ok := desired[featureRef]; ok {
480						t.Errorf("unexpected pack fetch including feature ref: %+v", desired)
481					}
482					return io.NopCloser(bytes.NewReader([]byte("PACK"))), nil
483				},
484			},
485			TargetPusher: fakeBootstrapPusher{
486				pushPack: func(_ context.Context, _ []gitproto.PushCommand, pack io.ReadCloser) error {
487					pushPackCalls++
488					_ = pack.Close()
489					return nil
490				},
491				pushCommands: func(_ context.Context, cmds []gitproto.PushCommand) error {
492					pushCommandsBatches = append(pushCommandsBatches, append([]gitproto.PushCommand(nil), cmds...))
493					return nil
494				},
495			},
496			DesiredRefs: map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef{
497				mainRef:    {SourceRef: mainRef, TargetRef: mainRef, SourceHash: mainHash, Kind: planner.RefKindBranch, Label: "main"},
498				featureRef: {SourceRef: featureRef, TargetRef: featureRef, SourceHash: featureHash, Kind: planner.RefKindBranch, Label: "feature"},
499			},
500			TargetRefs:       map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash{},
501			SourceHeadTarget: mainRef,
502			TargetMaxPack:    1024 * 1024,
503		}, "empty target")
504		if err != nil {
505			t.Fatalf("Execute: %v", err)
506		}
507	
508		if graphFetches != 1 {
509			t.Errorf("fetchCommitGraph called %d times, want 1 (trunk only)", graphFetches)
510		}
511		if packFetches != 1 {
512			t.Errorf("fetchPack called %d times, want 1 (trunk only)", packFetches)
513		}
514		if pushPackCalls != 1 {
515			t.Errorf("PushPack called %d times, want 1 (trunk only)", pushPackCalls)
516		}
517	
518		var foundFeatureCreate bool
519		for _, cmds := range pushCommandsBatches {
520			for _, cmd := range cmds {
521				if cmd.Name == featureRef && cmd.New == featureHash && cmd.Old == plumbing.ZeroHash && !cmd.Delete {
522					foundFeatureCreate = true
523				}
524			}
525		}
526		if !foundFeatureCreate {
527			t.Fatalf("expected ref-create command for feature at %s; got %v", featureHash, pushCommandsBatches)
528		}
529	}
530

532		fetchPack        func(context.Context, *gitproto.Conn, map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) (io.ReadCloser, error)

{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nPlan trunk first in batched bootstrap to cut per-branch graph fetches\n\nBefore: each branch's checkpoint planning fetched the full commit graph\nfrom the source independently. For a repo with 152 feature branches that\nall descend from main, that meant ~152 full-history commit-graph fetches\nand ~152 independent first-parent walks, each traversing the same shared\nhistory. On a linux-sized source this was ~40 s of wall time during\nplanning alone, before any object bytes moved.\n\nNow the trunk is identified up front via the source's HEAD symref, planned\nfirst, and its commit reachability is captured as a hash set. Each\nsubsequent branch's graph fetch passes trunk's tip as a have so the source\nreturns only the divergence, and the first-parent walk terminates when it\nenters trunk's ancestry instead of walking back to root.\n\nPieces:\n\n- gitproto: expose HEAD symref on RefService.HeadTarget. v1 reads it from\n  the symref capability; v2 adds \"symrefs\" to ls-refs and a \"ref-prefix\n  HEAD\" argument so HEAD is actually returned (otherwise ref-prefix\n  refs/heads/ filters it out and HeadTarget stays empty). HEAD itself is\n  consumed for the symref target and not surfaced in the ref slice,\n  matching v1 behavior where symbolic refs are filtered downstream.\n- gitproto: FetchCommitGraph takes an optional []plumbing.Hash of haves,\n  forwarded into the v2 fetch command. Nil/empty preserves prior behavior.\n- planner: add FirstParentChainStoppingAt that terminates the walk when a\n  commit is in a caller-supplied stop set. FirstParentChain becomes a thin\n  wrapper with nil stop set for existing callers.\n- bootstrap: orderTrunkFirst reorders DesiredRefs so the HEAD branch is\n  planned first; collectCommitHashes extracts just the commit hash set\n  (~8 B per commit, ~11 MB for linux) from trunk's graph store before it\n  is GC'd. Subsequent branches plan with trunk's tip as a have and\n  trunk's ancestry as a stop set. Emits one of three diagnostic log lines\n  at planning time so operators can see whether the fast path fired:\n  trunk selected, trunk unset (no HEAD advertised), or trunk unset (HEAD\n  filtered by --branch/--map). Subsumed branches (tip in trunk's\n  ancestry) emit a single-checkpoint batch today — the pack push for\n  those is a follow-up optimization.\n\nEmpty-chain edge case: when stop-at subsumes the branch tip,\nFirstParentChainStoppingAt returns nil and planCheckpointsFromChain\nfalls back to [tip] so the existing execute-phase logic still creates\nthe target ref.\n\nFalls back to the original per-branch behavior when HeadTarget is empty\nor when the trunk ref is filtered out of the desired set, so sources\nwithout symref advertisement and scoped runs (--branch, --map) are not\nregressed.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "commit 1"
}

{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nSkip pack push for branches fully subsumed by trunk\n\nWhen trunk is planned first, its ancestry set already tells us which\nbranches contain no new commits — their tip is already reachable from\ntrunk, so trunk's batches have delivered every object those branches\nneed. Previously those branches still ran the full checkpoint machinery:\na commit-graph fetch (usually near-empty), a pack fetch (usually\nnear-empty), a temp-ref push, a cutover push, and a temp-ref delete.\n\nWith this change, the subsumed case is detected in planBatches before the\ncommit-graph fetch, and the resulting plannedBatch carries a subsumed\nflag instead of checkpoints. executeBatched handles that flag with a\nsingle ref-create PushCommand that points at the source hash — no fetch,\nno pack, no temp ref, no cutover dance.\n\nCorrectness rests on trunkStopSet containing every commit reachable from\nthe trunk tip, not just the first-parent chain. collectCommitHashes\niterates all CommitObjects in the tree:0 fetch result, so merges and\ntheir ancestors are included. A branch tip is therefore only marked\nsubsumed when its entire history is already covered by trunk's batches.\n\nAdded TestExecuteBatchedSubsumedBranchSkipsPack verifies that for a\nfeature branch pointing at an older commit on trunk's chain, the planner\nemits zero commit-graph fetches and zero pack fetches for that branch,\nand the executor issues only a single ref-create via PushCommands.\n\nWebhook-count note: GitHub fires one push event per ref update, so the\nsubsumed branch still generates one webhook when its ref is created.\nThis change cuts the per-branch HTTP round-trips and object transfer for\nthat branch, not the webhook event itself. Operators concerned about\nwebhook volume during bulk migrations should still disable target\nwebhooks for the duration of the run.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "commit 2"
}
